Advaita Treatises

Upadeśasāhasrī

By Ādi Śaṅkara

Info

Includes 671 verse units and 116 prose units across 22 chapters. The opening ten verses have simple literal readings and word-for-word glosses; the other units do not yet have separate glosses.

Text source: Sengaku Mayeda, Śaṅkara’s Upadeśasāhasrī (1973; critical edition 2006)

Padyabhāga · Chapter 1: Caitanya-prakaraṇa

Verse I.1.1

I.1.1

चैतन्यं सर्वगं सर्वं सर्वभूतगुहाशयम् । यत् सर्वविषयातीतं तस्मै सर्वविदे नमः

Literal translation

Salutation to that all-knowing one: consciousness, all-pervading, all, dwelling in the heart-cave of every being, beyond every object.

Word-for-word
चैतन्यम् consciousness
सर्वगम् all-pervading
सर्वम् all
सर्व-भूत-गुहा-शयम् dwelling in the heart-cave of all beings
यत् which
सर्व-विषय-अतीतम् beyond every object
तस्मै to that
सर्व-विदे to the all-knowing one
नमः salutation
Expanded source translation

Salutations to that all-knowing One: all-pervading consciousness, dwelling in the hearts of all beings, and beyond every object of experience.

Verse I.1.2

I.1.2

समापय्य क्रियाः सर्वा दाराग्न्याधानपूर्विकाः । ब्रह्मविद्याम् अथेदानीं वक्तुं वेदः प्रचक्रमे

Literal translation

Having completed all ritual acts, beginning with marriage and establishing the sacred fires, the Veda now began to speak the knowledge of Brahman.

Word-for-word
समापय्य having completed
क्रियाः actions
सर्वाः all
दारा wives; marriage
अग्नि-आधान establishing the fires
पूर्विकाः beginning with
ब्रह्म-विद्याम् knowledge of Brahman
अथ then
इदानीम् now
वक्तुम् to speak
वेदः the Veda
प्रचक्रमे began
Expanded source translation

Having brought all ritual acts to an end, beginning with marriage and the establishing of sacred fires, the Veda now sets out to teach the knowledge of Brahman.

Verse I.1.3

I.1.3

कर्माणि देहयोगार्थं देहयोगे प्रियाप्रिये । ध्रुवे स्यातां ततो रागो द्वेषश् चैव ततः क्रियाः

Literal translation

Actions are for obtaining a body; with embodiment, pleasure and pain certainly arise. From them come attachment and aversion, and from these, actions.

Word-for-word
कर्माणि actions
देह-योग-अर्थम् for obtaining a body
देह-योगे in embodiment
प्रिय-अप्रिये pleasure and pain
ध्रुवे certain
स्याताम् arise
ततः from that
रागः attachment
द्वेषः aversion
च and
एव indeed
ततः from that
क्रियाः actions
Expanded source translation

Ritual actions are performed for the sake of obtaining a body; with embodiment come pleasure and pain, inevitably; from these arise attachment and aversion, and from them, actions.

Verse I.1.4

I.1.4

धर्माधर्मौ ततो ऽज्ञस्य देहयोगस् तथा पुनः एवं नित्यप्रवृत्तो ऽयं संसारश् चक्रवद् भृशम्

Literal translation

Then merit and demerit arise; for the ignorant, embodiment follows again. Thus this saṃsāra keeps turning continuously, like a wheel.

Word-for-word
धर्म-अधर्मौ merit and demerit
ततः from that
अज्ञस्य for the ignorant
देह-योगः embodiment
तथा thus
पुनः again
एवम् thus
नित्य-प्रवृत्तः continually turning
अयम् this
संसारः saṃsāra
चक्र-वत् like a wheel
भृशम् continuously
Expanded source translation

From action arise merit and demerit; for the ignorant these lead again to embodiment. Thus this cycle of worldly existence turns on without end, like a wheel.

Verse I.1.5

I.1.5

अज्ञानं तस्य मूलं स्याद् इति तद्धानम् इष्यते । ब्रह्मविद्यात आरब्धा ततो निःश्रेयसं भवेत्

Literal translation

Ignorance is its root; therefore its removal is sought. From undertaking the knowledge of Brahman, the highest good arises.

Word-for-word
अज्ञानम् ignorance
तस्य of it
मूलम् root
स्यात् would be
इति thus
तत्-हानम् removal of it
इष्यते is sought
ब्रह्म-विद्या knowledge of Brahman
आरब्धा undertaken
ततः from that
निःश्रेयसम् the highest good
भवेत् arises
Expanded source translation

Ignorance is its root, and its removal is therefore sought. Knowledge of Brahman is undertaken for that purpose; from it comes the highest good.

Verse I.1.6

I.1.6

विद्यैवाज्ञानहानाय न कर्माप्रतिकूलतः । नाज्ञानस्याप्रहाणे हि रागद्वेषक्षयो भवेत्

Literal translation

Knowledge alone is for the removal of ignorance; action is not its direct opposite. While ignorance remains, attachment and aversion do not cease.

Word-for-word
विद्या knowledge
एव alone
अज्ञान-हानाय for removal of ignorance
न not
कर्म action
अप्रतिकूलतः as its direct opposite
न not
अज्ञानस्य of ignorance
अप्रहाणे when not removed
हि indeed
राग-द्वेष-क्षयः cessation of attachment and aversion
भवेत् would occur
Expanded source translation

Knowledge alone removes ignorance; action is not its direct opposite. Until ignorance is removed, attachment and aversion cannot come to an end.

Verse I.1.7

I.1.7

रागद्वेषक्षयाभावे कर्म दोषोद्भवं ध्रुवम् । तस्मान् निःश्रेयसार्थाय विद्यैवात्र विधीयते

Literal translation

When attachment and aversion have not ceased, action inevitably gives rise to faults. Therefore knowledge alone is taught here for the highest good.

Word-for-word
राग-द्वेष-क्षय-अभावे when attachment and aversion have not ceased
कर्म action
दोष-उद्भवम् arising of faults
ध्रुवम् certainly
तस्मात् therefore
निःश्रेयस-अर्थाय for the highest good
विद्या knowledge
एव alone
अत्र here
विधीयते is prescribed
Expanded source translation

So long as attachment and aversion remain, action inevitably gives rise to faults. Therefore knowledge alone is taught here as the means to the highest good.

Verse I.1.8

I.1.8

ननु कर्म तथा नित्यं कर्तव्यं जीवने सति । विद्यायाः सहकारित्वं मोक्षं प्रति हि तद् व्रजेत्

Literal translation

Objection: As long as one lives, daily action must be done. Could action assist knowledge toward liberation?

Word-for-word
ननु objection
कर्म action
तथा thus
नित्यम् daily
कर्तव्यम् must be done
जीवने in living
सति while there is
विद्यायाः for knowledge
सहकारित्वम् assistance
मोक्षम् liberation
प्रति toward
हि indeed
तत् that
व्रजेत् could go
Expanded source translation

Objection: “As long as one lives, daily rites must be performed. Could action not assist knowledge in bringing about liberation?”

Verse I.1.9

I.1.9

यथा विद्या तथा कर्म चोदितत्वाविशेषतः । प्रत्यवायस्मृतेश् चैव कार्यं कर्म मुमुक्षुभिः

Literal translation

Objection: Knowledge and action are equally enjoined, and omission is remembered as a fault; therefore seekers of liberation must perform the action.

Word-for-word
यथा as
विद्या knowledge
तथा so
कर्म action
चोदितत्व-अविशेषतः because equally enjoined
प्रत्यवाय-स्मृतेः because a fault of omission is remembered
च and
एव indeed
कार्यम् must be done
कर्म action
मुमुक्षुभिः by seekers of liberation
Expanded source translation

“Knowledge and action are alike prescribed by scripture; omission of the prescribed rite is also remembered as a fault. Seekers of liberation should therefore perform their duties.”

Verse I.1.10

I.1.10

ननु ध्रुवफला विद्या नान्यत् किंचिद् अपेक्षते । नाग्निष्टोमो यथैवान्यद् ध्रुवकार्यो ऽप्य् अपेक्षते

Literal translation

Objection: Knowledge has a certain result and depends on nothing else. The Agniṣṭoma rite also has a certain result, yet it depends on other factors.

Word-for-word
ननु objection
ध्रुव-फलाः having a certain result
विद्या knowledge
न not
अन्यत् other
किञ्चित् anything
अपेक्षते depends on
न not
अग्निष्टोमः the Agniṣṭoma rite
यथा as
एव also
अन्यत् other factors
ध्रुव-कार्यः having a certain result
अपेक्षते depends on
Expanded source translation

Objection: “Knowledge has a certain result and depends on nothing else. Yet even the certain result of the Agniṣṭoma rite requires other factors.”

Verse I.1.11

I.1.11

तथा ध्रुवफला विद्या कर्म नित्यम् अपेक्षते । इत्य् एवं केचिद् इच्छन्ति न कर्म प्रतिकूलतः

“In the same way, even though its result is certain, knowledge depends on the regular rites.” Some hold this view, though action is not directly opposed to knowledge.

Verse I.1.12

I.1.12

विद्यायाः प्रतिकूलं हि कर्म स्यात् साभिमानतः । निर्विकारात्मबुद्धिश् च विद्येतीह प्रकीर्तिता

Action, performed with the sense of agency, is opposed to knowledge. Knowledge is declared here to be understanding of the changeless Self.

Verse I.1.13

I.1.13

अहं कर्ता ममेदं स्याद् इति कर्म प्रवर्तते । वस्त्वधीना भवेद् विद्या कर्त्रधीनो भवेद् विधिः

Action proceeds from the thought ‘I am the doer; this is mine.’ Knowledge depends on the reality as it is; an injunction depends on an agent.

Verse I.1.14

I.1.14

कारकाण्य् उपमृद्नाति विद्याब्बुद्धिम् इवोषरे । तत्सत्यमतिम् आदाय कर्म कर्तुं व्यवस्यति

Knowledge crushes the factors of action, as insight dries up in barren ground. Taking truth to be established, [the opponent] resolves to perform action.

Verse I.1.15

I.1.15

विरुद्धत्वाद् अतः शक्यं कर्म कर्तुं न विद्यया । सहैवं विदुषा तस्मात् कर्म हेयं मुमुक्षुणा

Because they are opposed, action cannot be performed together with knowledge. Therefore the seeker of liberation should renounce action.

Verse I.1.16

I.1.16

देहाद्यैर् अविशेषेण देहिनो ग्रहणं निजम् । प्राणिनां तद् अविद्योत्थं तावत् कर्मविधिर् भवेत्

Living beings identify themselves without distinction with the body and the rest. That identification arises from ignorance; on it rests the injunction to act.

Verse I.1.17

I.1.17

नेति नेतीति देहादीन् अपोह्यात्मावशेषितः । अविशेषात्मबोधार्थं तेनाविद्या निवर्तिता

By the teaching ‘not this, not this,’ the body and the rest are set aside and the Self alone remains. Knowledge of the undifferentiated Self removes ignorance.

Verse I.1.18

I.1.18

निवृत्ता सा कथं भूयः प्रसूयेत प्रमाणतः । असत्य् एवाविशेषे हि प्रत्यगात्मनि केवले

How could ignorance, once removed, arise again through a means of knowledge, when only the one inward Self, without distinction, is real?

Verse I.1.19

I.1.19

न चेद् भूयः प्रसूयेत कर्ता भोक्तेति धीः कथम् । सद् अस्मीति च विज्ञाने तस्माद् विद्यासहायिका

If it does not arise again, how could the thought ‘I am the doer and enjoyer’ return? When one knows ‘I am Being,’ knowledge alone supports knowledge.

Verse I.1.20

I.1.20

अत्यरेचयद् इत्य् उक्तो न्यासः श्रुत्यात एव हि । कर्मभ्यो मानसान्तेभ्य एतावद् इति वाजिनाम्

The Veda itself says that renunciation surpasses [all other paths], and that the Vājasaneyins teach this: all action, even mental action, is set aside.

Verse I.1.21

I.1.21

अमृतत्वं श्रुतं तस्मात् त्याज्यं कर्म मुमुक्षुभिः । अग्निष्टोमवद् इत्य् उक्तं तत्रेदम् अभिधीयते

Immortality is taught as the result; therefore seekers of liberation should renounce action. The Agniṣṭoma comparison was raised, and this is the reply:

Verse I.1.22

I.1.22

नैककारकसाध्यत्वात् फलान्यत्वाच् च कर्मणः । विद्या तद्विपरीतातो दृष्टान्तो विषमो भवेत्

“Because action depends on many factors and has a different result, it is unlike knowledge, which is the opposite; the example is therefore not comparable.”

Verse I.1.23

I.1.23

कृष्यादिवत् फलार्थत्वाद् अन्यकर्मोपबृंहणम् । अग्निष्टोमस् त्व् अपेक्षेत विद्यान्यत् किम् अपेक्षते

“Other actions support an action that produces a result, as farming does; the Agniṣṭoma depends on them. What else does knowledge depend on?”

Verse I.1.24

I.1.24

प्रत्यवायस् तु तस्यैव यस्याहंकार इष्यते । अहंकारफलार्थित्वे विद्येते नात्मवेदिनः

The fault of omission belongs only to one who maintains the ego. For a knower of the Self there is no knowledge of an ego seeking results.

Verse I.1.25

I.1.25

तस्माद् अज्ञानहानाय संसारविनिवृत्तये । ब्रह्मविद्याविधानाय प्रारब्धोपनिषत् त्व् इयम्

Therefore this Upaniṣad begins, to remove ignorance and end worldly existence, teaching the knowledge of Brahman.

Verse I.1.26

I.1.26

सदेर् उपनिपूर्वस्य क्विपि चोपनिषद् भवेत् । मन्दीकरणभावाच् च गर्भादेः शातनात् तथा

The word *upaniṣad* is derived from the root *sad* with the prefixes *upa* and *ni* and the suffix *kvip*. It is so called because it weakens bondage and loosens the cycle of birth, beginning with the womb.

Padyabhāga · Chapter 2: Pratiṣedha-prakaraṇa

Verse I.2.1

I.2.1

प्रतिषेद्धुम् अशक्यत्वान् नेति नेतीति शेषितम् । इदं नाहम् इदं नाहम् इत्य् अद्धा प्रतिपद्यते

Since it cannot be denied, the teaching ‘not this, not this’ is retained; one directly understands, ‘I am not this; I am not this.’

Verse I.2.2

I.2.2

इदंधीर् इदमात्मोत्था वाचारम्भणगोचरा । निषिद्धात्मोद्भवत्वात् सा न पुनर् मानतां व्रजेत्

The cognition ‘this’ arises from the Self and concerns what can be expressed in words. Since it arises from what has been denied as the Self, it cannot itself serve as a valid means of knowledge.

Verse I.2.3

I.2.3

पूर्वबुद्धिम् अबाधित्वा नोत्तरा जायते मतिः । दृशिर् एकः स्वयंसिद्धः फलत्वात् स न बाध्यते

A later cognition does not arise without sublating the earlier one. The Seer alone is self-established; as the ground of every result, it cannot be sublated.

Verse I.2.4

I.2.4

इदंवनम् अतिक्रम्य शोकमोहादिदूषितम् । वनाद् गान्धारको यद्वत् स्वम् आत्मानं प्रपद्यते

“Just as the man from Gandhāra, leaving the forest of grief and delusion, finds his way back to himself,”

Padyabhāga · Chapter 3: Īśvara-prakaraṇa

Verse I.3.1

I.3.1

ईश्वरश् चेद् अनात्मा स्यान् नासाव् अस्मीति धारयेत् । आत्मा चेद् ईश्वरो ऽस्मीति विद्या सान्यनिवर्तिका

“if the Lord were not the Self, one could not hold the thought ‘I am He.’ If the Self is the Lord, knowledge of that fact removes all else.”

Verse I.3.2

I.3.2

आत्मनो ऽन्यस्य चेद् धर्मा अस्थूलत्वादयो मताः । अज्ञेयत्वे ऽस्य किं तैः स्याद् आत्मत्वे त्व् अन्यधीह्नुतिः

If qualities such as ‘not gross’ belong to something other than the Self, what use are they if that reality cannot be known? If they belong to the Self, they dispel the notion of another.

Verse I.3.3

I.3.3

मिथ्याध्यासनिषेधार्थं ततो ऽस्थूलादि गृह्यताम् । परत्र चेन् निषेधार्थं शून्यतावर्णनं हि तत्

To deny false superimposition, understand the teaching ‘not gross’ and the rest. If it were meant to deny some other thing, it would merely describe a void.

Verse I.3.4

I.3.4

बुभुत्सोर् यदि चान्यत्र प्रत्यगात्मन इष्यते । अप्राणो ह्य् अमनाः शुभ्र इति चानर्थकं वचः

If the object to be known were held to be elsewhere than the innermost Self, the words ‘without breath, without mind, pure’ would have no purpose.

Padyabhāga · Chapter 4: Ahaṃpratyaya-prakaraṇa

Verse I.4.1

I.4.1

अहंप्रत्ययबीजं यद् अहंप्रत्ययवत्स्थितम् । नाहंप्रत्ययवह्न्युष्टं कथं कर्म प्ररोहति

“If the seed of the thought ‘I’ remains, and stands as the source of that thought, how can action sprout when the fire of ‘I’-awareness has scorched it?”

Verse I.4.2

I.4.2

दृष्टवच् चेत् प्ररोहः स्यान् नान्यकर्मा स इष्यते । तन्निरोधे कथं तत् स्यात् पृच्छामो वस् तद् उच्यताम्

“If action sprouts, as it appears to, it is said to be some other action. How could it remain when that source is destroyed? We ask you: explain this.”

Verse I.4.3

I.4.3

देहाद्यारम्भसामर्थ्याज् ज्ञानं सद्विषयं त्वयि । अभिभूय फलं कुर्यात् कर्मान्ते ज्ञानम् उद्भवेत्

“Because it has the capacity to produce a body and the rest, knowledge in you concerns what is real; it suppresses action and bears fruit, and when the action ends, knowledge arises.”

Verse I.4.4

I.4.4

आरब्धस्य फले ह्य् एते भोगो ज्ञानं च कर्मणः । अविरोधस् तयोर् युक्तो वैधर्म्यं चेतरस्य तु

“For action already begun, both experience and knowledge are its results. Their coexistence is reasonable; other kinds of action differ.”

Verse I.4.5

I.4.5

देहात्मज्ञानवज् ज्ञानं देहात्मज्ञानबाधकम् । आत्मन्य् एय भवेद् यस्य स नेच्छन्न् अपि मुच्यते

“Knowledge, like the knowledge ‘I am the body,’ can sublate the knowledge ‘I am the body.’ Whoever has this knowledge established in the Self is liberated, even without intending it.”

Padyabhāga · Chapter 5: Mūtrāśaṅka-prakaraṇa

Verse I.5.1

I.5.1

मूत्राशङ्को यथोदङ्को नाग्रहीद् अमृतं मुनिः । कर्मनाशभयाज् जन्तोर् आत्मज्ञानाग्रहस् तथा

“The sage Udaṅka, fearing the loss of ritual merit, would not accept nectar, mistaking it for urine; in the same way a person refuses knowledge of the Self for fear of losing the results of action.”

Verse I.5.2

I.5.2

बुद्धिस्थश् चलतीवात्मा ध्यायतीव च दृश्यते । नौगतस्य यथा वृक्षास् तद्वत् संसारविभ्रमः

“The Self appears to move and think because it is reflected in the intellect, just as trees seem to move to one travelling in a boat.”

Verse I.5.3

I.5.3

नौस्थस्य प्रातिलोम्येन नगानां गमनं यथा । आत्मनः संसृतिस् तद्वद् ध्यायतीवेति हि श्रुतिः

“As trees seem to move backward to someone in a boat, so the Self seems to undergo worldly change, says scripture, using ‘as if it thinks.’”

Verse I.5.4

I.5.4

चैतन्यप्रतिबिम्बेन व्याप्तो बोधो हि जायते । बुद्धेः शब्दादिनिर्भासस् तेन मोमुह्यते जगत्

“Awareness, reflected in consciousness, pervades the intellect; through it, sounds and the rest appear there, and the world is deluded.”

Verse I.5.5

I.5.5

चैतन्याभासताहमस् तादर्थ्यं च तद् अस्य यत् । इदमंशप्रहाणे न परः सो ऽनुभवो भवेत्

“The ‘I’-thought is a reflection of awareness and takes on its nature. When the ‘this’ aspect is relinquished, the supreme experience remains.”

Padyabhāga · Chapter 6: Chittvā-prakaraṇa

Verse I.6.1

I.6.1

छित्त्वा त्यक्तेन हस्तेन स्वयं नात्मा विशेष्यते । तथा शिष्टेन सर्वेण येन येन विशेष्यते

“A person is not defined by a hand once it has been cut off and discarded; in the same way, by whatever remains, the Self is distinguished.”

Verse I.6.2

I.6.2

तस्मात् त्यक्तेन हस्तेन तुल्यं सर्वं विशेषणम् । अनात्मत्वेन तस्माज् ज्ञो मुक्तः सर्वविशेषणैः

“Every attribute is therefore like the discarded hand. Knowing it as not-Self, the knower is free of every attribute.”

Verse I.6.3

I.6.3

विशेषणम् इदं सर्वं साध्वलंकरणं यथा । अविद्यास्तम् अतः सर्वं ज्ञात आत्मन्य् असद् भवेत्

“All these attributes are like ornaments; once ignorance has ended, everything known as an attribute is absent in the Self.”

Verse I.6.4

I.6.4

ज्ञातैवात्मा सदा ग्राह्यो ज्ञेयम् उत्सृज्य केवलः । अहम् इत्य् अपि यद् ग्राह्यं व्यपेताङ्गसमं हि तत्

“The knower alone is always to be recognized as the Self, after setting aside what is known. Even what can be grasped as ‘I’ is like a limb that has been removed.”

Verse I.6.5

I.6.5

यावान् स्याद् इदमंशो यः स स्वतो ऽन्यो विशेषणम् । विशेषप्रक्षयो यत्र सिद्धो ज्ञश् चित्रगुर् यथा

“Whatever portion of ‘this’ there may be is an attribute, other than the Self; when every distinction is ended, the knower is established, like a person who has lost his limbs.”

Verse I.6.6

I.6.6

इदम् अंशो ऽहम् इत्य् अत्र त्याज्यो नात्मेति पण्डितैः । अहं ब्रह्मेति शिष्टो ऽंशो भूतपूर्वगतेर् भवेत्

“The wise should set aside the ‘this’ portion and the thought ‘I’ as not-Self; what remains is the portion expressed by ‘I am Brahman,’ grounded in the original Self.”

Padyabhāga · Chapter 7: Buddhyārūḍha-prakaraṇa

Verse I.7.1

I.7.1

बुद्ध्यारूढं सदा सर्वं दृश्यते यत्र तत्र वा । मया तस्मात् परं ब्रह्म सर्वज्ञश् चास्मि सर्वगः

“Everything, everywhere, is seen in the intellect; therefore I am the supreme Brahman, all-knowing and all-pervading.”

Verse I.7.2

I.7.2

यथात्मबुद्धिचाराणां साक्षी तद्वत् परेष्व् अपि । नैवापोढुं न वादातुं शक्यस् तस्मात् परो ह्य् अहम्

“I am the witness of the movements of my own intellect; likewise of the intellects of others. I cannot be set aside or denied by argument; therefore I am supreme.”

Verse I.7.3

I.7.3

विकारित्वम् अशुद्धत्वं भौतिकत्वं न चात्मनः । अशेषबुद्धिसाक्षित्वाद् बुद्धिवच् चाल्पवेदना

“The Self is not subject to change, impurity or material nature. As witness of every movement of the intellect, it knows no less than the intellect.”

Verse I.7.4

I.7.4

मणौ प्रकाश्यते यद्वद् रक्ताद्याकारतातपे । मयि संदृश्यते सर्वम् आतपेनेव तन् मया

“Just as red and other colors appear in a jewel when sunlight falls on it, everything appears in me, as objects appear in sunlight.”

Verse I.7.5

I.7.5

बुद्धौ दृश्यं भवेद् बुद्धौ सत्यां नास्ति विपर्यये । द्रष्टा यस्मात् सदा द्रष्टा तस्माद् द्वैतं न विद्यते

“What is seen exists when the intellect exists and is absent when it does not. Since the Seer is always the Seer, duality does not exist.”

Verse I.7.6

I.7.6

अविवेकात् पराभावं यथा बुद्धिर् अवेत् तथा । विवेकात् तु पराद् अन्यः स्वयं चापि न विद्यते

“Through lack of discrimination the intellect imagines another reality; through discrimination, no other than the supreme Self exists, nor does the Self become something else.”

Padyabhāga · Chapter 8: Citisvarūpa-prakaraṇa

Verse I.8.1

I.8.1

चितिस्वरूपं स्वत एव मे मते रसादियोगस् तव मोहकारितः । अतो न किंचित् तव चेष्टितेन मे फलं भवेत् सर्वविशेषहानतः

“By nature I am pure consciousness; your connection with taste and the rest is caused by delusion. Since every distinction is relinquished, nothing you do can produce a result for me.”

Verse I.8.2

I.8.2

विमुच्य मायामयकार्यताम् इह प्रशान्तिम् आयाह्य् असदीहितात् सदा । अहं परं ब्रह्म सदा विमुक्तवत् तथाजम् एकं द्वयवर्जितं यतः

“Free yourself from the effects fashioned by māyā and always come to peace, away from false desires. I am ever-free, unborn, one and without a second—the supreme Brahman.”

Verse I.8.3

I.8.3

सदा च भूतेषु समो ऽस्मि केवलो यथा च खं सर्वगम् अक्षरं शिवम् । निरन्तरं निष्कलम् अक्रियं परं ततो न मे ऽस्तीह फलं तवेहितैः

“I am always equal in all beings, alone, all-pervading and imperishable like space, auspicious, without parts, actionless and supreme. Therefore your deeds bring me no result.”

Verse I.8.4

I.8.4

अहं ममैको न तदन्यद् इष्यते तथा न कस्याप्य् अहम् अस्म्य् असङ्गतः । असङ्गरूपो ऽहम् अतो न मे त्वया कृतेन कार्यं तव चाद्वयत्वतः

“I alone am; nothing else is desired. I belong to no one, being unattached. My nature is nonattachment, so nothing is to be done by what you do, for you and I are nondual.”

Verse I.8.5

I.8.5

फले च हेतौ च जनो विषक्तवान् इति प्रचिन्त्याहम् अतो विमोक्षणे । जनस्य संवादम् इमं प्रकॢप्तवान् स्वरूपतत्त्वार्थविबोधकारणम्

“Seeing that people are attached to actions and their fruits, I have composed this dialogue to free them and reveal the truth of their own nature.”

Verse I.8.6

I.8.6

संवादम् एतं यदि चिन्तयेन् नरो विमुच्यते ऽज्ञानमहाभयागमात् । विमुक्तकामश् च तथा जनः सदा चरत्य् अशोकः सम आत्मवित् सुखी

“A person who reflects on this dialogue is freed from the great terror of ignorance. Released from desire, the knower of the Self lives always without sorrow, in equanimity and happiness.”

Padyabhāga · Chapter 9: Sūkṣmatā-prakaraṇa

Verse I.9.1

I.9.1

सूक्ष्मताव्यापिते ज्ञेये गन्धादेर् उत्तरोत्तरम् । प्रत्यगात्मावसानेषु पूर्वपूर्वप्रहाणतः

“The knowable becomes progressively subtler and more pervasive, from smell onward; by giving up each earlier element, one comes to the innermost Self.”

Verse I.9.2

I.9.2

शारीरा पृथिवी तावद् यावद् बाह्या प्रमाणतः । अबादीनि च तत्त्वानि तावज् ज्ञेयानि कृत्स्नशः

“The body made of earth extends only as far as external evidence reaches; in the same way, the elements beginning with water are each to be understood completely.”

Verse I.9.3

I.9.3

वाय्वादीनां यथोत्पत्तेः पूर्वं खं सर्वगं तथा । अहम् एकः सदा सर्वश् चिन्मात्रः सर्वगो ऽद्वयः

“As space pervades all before the emergence of air and the other elements, so I alone am always all: pure consciousness, all-pervading and nondual.”

Verse I.9.4

I.9.4

ब्रह्माद्याः स्थावरान्ता ये प्राणिनो मम पूः स्मृताः । कामक्रोधादयो दोषा जायेरन् मे कुतो ऽन्यतः

“All beings, from Brahmā down to the motionless, are said to be my dwelling. From what other source could faults such as desire and anger arise in me?”

Verse I.9.5

I.9.5

भूतदोषैः सदास्पृष्टं सर्वभूतस्थम् ईश्वरम् । नीलं व्योम यथा बालो दुष्टं मां वीक्षते जनः

“Though untouched by the defects of beings, the Lord dwells in every being. Like a child who sees the clear sky as blue, a person sees me as tainted.”

Verse I.9.6

I.9.6

मच्चैतन्यावभास्यत्वात् सर्वप्राणिधियां सदा । पूर् मम प्राणिनः सर्वे सर्वज्ञस्य विपाप्मनः

“Because I am the awareness in which every living being’s intellect appears, all beings are my dwelling; I am all-knowing and free from evil.”

Verse I.9.7

I.9.7

जनिमज् ज्ञानविज्ञेयं स्वप्नज्ञानवद् इष्यते । नित्यं निर्विषयं ज्ञानं तस्माद् द्वैतं न विद्यते

“Knowledge that arises and is known is like the knowledge of a dream. Knowledge that is eternal has no object; therefore duality does not exist.”

Verse I.9.8

I.9.8

ज्ञातुर् ज्ञातिर् हि नित्योक्ता सुषुप्ते त्व् अन्यशून्यतः । जाग्रज्ज्ञातिस् त्व् अविद्यातस् तद् ग्राह्यं चासद् इष्यताम्

“The knower’s awareness is said to be eternal; in deep sleep there is no second thing. Waking awareness depends on ignorance, so what it grasps may be regarded as unreal.”

Verse I.9.9

I.9.9

रूपवत्त्वाद्यसत्त्वान् न दृष्ट्यादेः कर्मता यथा । एवं विज्ञानकर्मत्वं भूम्नो नास्तीति गम्यते

“Just as sight itself does not become an action because it lacks form and the other properties, so the fullness of awareness is not an object of cognition.”

Padyabhāga · Chapter 10: Dṛśi-prakaraṇa

Verse I.10.1

I.10.1

दृशिस्वरूपं गगनोपमं परं सकृद्विभातं त्व् अजम् एकम् अक्षरम् । अलेपकं सर्वगतं यद् अद्वयं तद् एव चाहं सततं विमुक्त ओम्

“I am the supreme witnessing nature, like space, shining all at once, unborn, one, imperishable, unstained and all-pervading. I am nondual and always free. Om.”

Verse I.10.2

I.10.2

दृशिस् तु शुद्धो ऽहम् अविक्रियात्मको न मे ऽस्ति कश्चिद् विषयः स्वभावतः । पुरस् तिरश् चोर्ध्वम् अधश् च सर्वतः सुपूर्णभूमा त्व् अज आत्मनि स्थितः

“I am pure awareness, unchanging by nature. No object is my nature. Before, behind, above, below and everywhere, I am the fullness, unborn and established in the Self.”

Verse I.10.3

I.10.3

अजो ऽमरश् चैव तथाजरो ऽमृतः स्वयंप्रभः सर्वगतो ऽहम् अद्वयः । न कारणं कार्यम् अतीव निर्मलः सदैव तृप्तश् च ततो विमुक्त ओम्

“I am unborn, undying, ageless, immortal, self-luminous, all-pervading and nondual; perfectly pure, beyond cause and effect, always fulfilled, and therefore free. Om.”

Verse I.10.4

I.10.4

सुषुप्तजाग्रत्स्वपतश् च दर्शनं न मे ऽस्ति किंचित् स्वम् इवेह मोहनम् । स्वतश् च तेषां परतो ऽप्य् असत्त्वतस् तुरीय एवास्मि सदा दृग् अद्वयः

“I have no experience of anything as my own in deep sleep, waking or dream, which are delusive. Since they are unreal in themselves and through something else, I am ever the Fourth: the nondual Seer.”

Verse I.10.5

I.10.5

शरीरबुद्धीन्द्रियदुःखसंततिर् न मे न चाहं मम निर्विकारतः । असत्त्वहेतोश् च तथैव संततेर् असत्त्वम् अस्याः स्वपतो हि दृश्यवत्

“The body, intellect, senses and stream of suffering are not mine; because I am changeless, I am not them. Like appearances in a dream, this stream is unreal because it has no real ground.”

Verse I.10.6

I.10.6

इदं तु सत्यं मम नास्ति विक्रिया विकारहेतुर् न हि मे ऽद्वयत्वतः । न पुण्यपापे न च मोक्षबन्धने न चास्ति वर्णाश्रमताशरीरतः

“There is no change in me; this is true. Because I am nondual, I have no cause of change. I have neither merit nor demerit, bondage nor liberation, nor caste, stage of life or body.”

Verse I.10.7

I.10.7

अनादितो निर्गुणतो न कर्म मे फलं च तस्मात् परमो ऽहम् अद्वयः । यथा नभः सर्वगतं न लिप्यते तथा ह्य् अहं देहगतो ऽपि सूक्ष्मतः

“I am without beginning and without qualities; I have no action and therefore no result. I am supreme and nondual. Like space, I remain unstained though subtly present in the body.”

Verse I.10.8

I.10.8

सदा च भूतेषु समो ऽहम् ईश्वरः क्षराक्षराभ्यां परमो ह्य् अथोत्तमः । परात्मतत्त्वश् च तथाद्वयो ऽपि सन् विपर्ययेणाभिवृतस् त्व् अविद्यया

“I am the Lord, equal in all beings, beyond the perishable and the imperishable, highest, and the nondual truth of the supreme Self; yet ignorance covers me through a mistaken appearance.”

Verse I.10.9

I.10.9

अविद्यया भावनया च कर्मभिर् विविक्त आत्माव्यवधिः सुनिर्मलः । दृगादिशक्तिप्रचितो ऽहम् अद्वयः स्थितः स्वरूपे गगनं यथाचलम्

“Distinct, pure and without limit, I am awareness alone, filled with the power of seeing and the rest. Ignorance and its imaginings do not touch me; I remain in my own nature, unmoving like space.”

Verse I.10.10

I.10.10

अहं परं ब्रह्म विनिश्चयात्मदृङ् न जायते भूय इति श्रुतेर् वचः । न चैव बीजे त्व् असति प्रजायते फलं न जन्मास्ति ततो ह्य् अमोहता

“I am the supreme Brahman”—this is the Self’s decisive vision; scripture says one is not born again. Without a seed, no fruit arises, and so there is no rebirth when delusion is gone.”

Verse I.10.11

I.10.11

ममेदम् इत्थं च तवाद ईदृशं तथाहम् एवं न परो ऽपि चान्यथा । विमूढतैवास्य जनस्य कल्पना सदा समे ब्रह्मणि वाद्वये शिवे

“‘This is mine,’ ‘you are this,’ ‘I am this,’ and ‘another is otherwise’—such imaginings belong to the deluded person, in the equal, auspicious, nondual Brahman.”

Verse I.10.12

I.10.12

यद् अद्वयं ज्ञानम् अतीव निर्मलं महात्मनां तत्र न शोकमोहता । तयोर् अभावे न हि कर्म जन्म वा भवेद् अयं वेदविदां विनिश्चयः

“In the perfectly pure, nondual knowledge of the great Self there is no grief or delusion. When both are absent, there can be no action or birth; this is the settled understanding of those who know the Veda.”

Verse I.10.13

I.10.13

सुषुप्तवज् जाग्रति यो न पश्यति द्वयं तु पश्यन्न् अपि चाद्वयत्वतः । तथा च कुर्वन्न् अपि निष्क्रियश् च यः स आत्मविन् नान्य इतीह निश्चयः

“One who, awake, sees no duality as in deep sleep, and who, even while seeing, sees nondually; who acts and yet is actionless—that one is a knower of the Self, none other.”

Verse I.10.14

I.10.14

इतीदम् उक्तं परमार्थदर्शनं मया हि वेदान्तविनिश्चितं परम् । विमुच्यते ऽस्मिन् यदि निश्चितो भवेन् न लिप्यते व्योमवद् एव कर्मभिः

“This highest vision of reality, established by Vedānta, has been taught by me. Whoever becomes certain of it is freed and is not stained by actions, as space is unstained.”

Padyabhāga · Chapter 11: Īkṣitṛtva-prakaraṇa

Verse I.11.1

I.11.1

ईक्षितृत्वं स्वतःसिद्धं जन्तूनां च ततो ऽन्यता । अज्ञानाद् इत्य् अतो ऽन्यत्वं सद् असीति निवर्त्यते

“The sense of agency is self-established, and the distinction from other beings arises through ignorance. The words ‘You are that’ remove this apparent otherness.”

Verse I.11.2

I.11.2

एतावद् ध्य् अमृतत्वं न किंचिद् अन्यत् सहायकम् । ज्ञानस्येति ब्रुवच् छास्त्रं सलिङ्गं कर्म बाधते

“This alone is immortality; nothing else assists it. Scripture, teaching that knowledge is sufficient, refutes action and its accompanying rites.”

Verse I.11.3

I.11.3

सर्वेषां मनसो वृत्तम् अविशेषेण पश्यतः । तस्य मे निर्विकारस्य विशेषः स्यात् कथंचन

“I see without distinction the activity of every mind. How could there be any difference in me, who am changeless?”

Verse I.11.4

I.11.4

मनोवृत्तं मनश् चैव स्वप्नवज् जाग्रतीक्षितुः । संप्रसादे द्वयासत्त्वाच् चिन्मात्रः सर्वगो ऽद्वयः

“For the Seer in waking, the mind’s activity and the mind itself are like a dream. In deep repose, when neither is present, I am pure consciousness, all-pervading and nondual.”

Verse I.11.5

I.11.5

स्वप्नः सत्यो यथाबोधाद् देहात्मत्वं तथैव च । प्रत्यक्षादेः प्रमाणत्वं जाग्रत् स्याद् आत्मवेदनात्

“A dream seems real until awakening; so too does identification of the Self with the body. Perception and the other means of knowledge seem valid in waking until knowledge of the Self.”

Verse I.11.6

I.11.6

व्योमवत् सर्वभूतस्थो भूतदोषैर् विवर्जितः । साक्षी चेतागुणः शुद्धो ब्रह्मैवास्मीति केवलः

“Like space, I dwell in all beings and am free of their defects. I am the witness, conscious, without qualities, pure, and none other than Brahman.”

Verse I.11.7

I.11.7

नामरूपक्रियाभ्यो ऽन्यो नित्यमुक्तस्वरूपवान् । अहम् आत्मा परं ब्रह्म चिन्मात्रो ऽहं सदाद्वयः

“Beyond name, form and action, eternally free in nature, I am the Self, the supreme Brahman, pure consciousness, ever nondual.”

Verse I.11.8

I.11.8

अहं ब्रह्मास्मि कर्ता च भोक्ता चास्मीति ये विदुः । ते नष्टा ज्ञनकर्मभ्यां नास्तिकाः स्युर् न संशयः

“Those who think ‘I am Brahman’ and at the same time ‘I am the doer and enjoyer’ are lost to both knowledge and action; without doubt, they are nihilists.”

Verse I.11.9

I.11.9

धर्माधर्मफलैर् योग इष्टो ऽदृष्टो यथात्मनः । शास्त्राद् ब्रह्मत्वम् अप्य् अस्य मोक्षो ज्ञानात् तथेष्यताम्

“As the Self is connected with the unseen fruits of merit and demerit, so let Brahmanhood be accepted on the authority of scripture and liberation on the authority of knowledge.”

Verse I.11.10

I.11.10

या माहारजनाद्यास् ता वासनाः स्वप्नदर्शिभिः । अनुभूयन्त एवेह ततो ऽन्यः केवलो दृशिः

“Those who dream experience impressions such as garlands and the rest; therefore I am the pure Seer, other than these.”

Verse I.11.11

I.11.11

कोशाद् इव विनिष्कृष्टः कार्यकारणवर्जितः । यथासिर् दृश्यते स्वप्ने तद्वद् बोद्धा स्वयंप्रभः

“Like a sword drawn out from its sheath, the knower is seen in a dream as free of cause and effect, self-luminous.”

Verse I.11.12

I.11.12

आपेषात् प्रतिबुद्धस्य ज्ञस्य स्वाभाविकं पदम् । उक्तं नेत्यादिवाक्येन कल्पितस्यापनेतृणा

“The natural state of the awakened knower, from the moment of awakening onward, is taught by the words ‘not this, not this,’ which remove what was imagined.”

Verse I.11.13

I.11.13

महाराजादयो लोका मयि यद्वत् प्रकल्पिताः । स्वप्ने तद्वद् द्वयं विद्याद् रूपं वासनया सह

“Just as great kings and other worlds are imagined in me, so in a dream one should understand the two—the visible form and its latent impression—as imagined.”

Verse I.11.14

I.11.14

देहलिङ्गात्मना कार्या वासनारूपिणा क्रिया । नेतिनेत्यात्मरूपत्वान् न मे कार्या क्रिया क्वचित्

“Action belongs to the body and subtle body, arising from impressions; I have no action at any time, for my nature is taught as ‘not this, not this.’”

Verse I.11.15

I.11.15

न ततो ऽमृतताशास्ति कर्मणो ऽज्ञानहेतुतः । मोक्षस्य ज्ञानहेतुत्वान् न तदन्यद् अपेक्षते

“Action cannot bring immortality, since ignorance is its cause; liberation comes from knowledge and depends on nothing else.”

Verse I.11.16

I.11.16

अमृतं चाभयं नार्तं नेतीत्यात्मा प्रियो मम । विपरीतम् अतो ऽन्यद् यत् त्यजेत् तत् सक्रियं ततः

“The Self, taught as immortal, fearless and free of sorrow by ‘not this, not this,’ is dear to me. Whatever is its opposite should be abandoned as bound to action.”

Padyabhāga · Chapter 12: Prakāśastha-prakaraṇa

Verse I.12.1

I.12.1

प्रकाशस्थं यथा देहं सालोकम् अभिमन्यते । द्रष्ट्राभासं तथा चित्तं द्रष्टाहम् इति मन्यते

“Just as a body in light is imagined to shine by its own light, so the mind, reflecting the Seer, imagines ‘I am the Seer.’”

Verse I.12.2

I.12.2

यद् एव दृष्यते लोके तेनाभिन्नत्वम् आत्मनः । प्रपद्यते ततो मूढस् तेनात्मानं न विन्दति

“The ignorant person identifies the Self with whatever is seen in the world; because of this, that person does not find the Self.”

Verse I.12.3

I.12.3

दशमस्य नवात्मत्वप्रतिपत्तिवद् आत्मनः । दृश्येषु तद्वद् एवायं मूढो लोको न चान्यथा

“Just as someone who counts the tenth person among the nine mistakes him for someone else, so too this deluded world mistakes the Self among visible things.”

Verse I.12.4

I.12.4

त्वं कुरु त्वं तद् एवेति प्रत्ययाव् एककालिकौ । एकनीडौ कथं स्यातां विरुद्धौ न्यायतो वद

“‘You act’ and ‘you are that’—how can these two judgments, contradictory yet belonging to the same person, be true at the same time? Explain the reasoning.”

Verse I.12.5

I.12.5

देहाभिमानिनो दुःखं नादेहस्य स्वभावतः । स्वापवत् तत्प्रहाणाय तत् त्वम् इत्य् उच्यते दृशेः

“Sorrow belongs to one who identifies with the body; it is not the nature of the bodiless Self. As in a dream, the teaching ‘you are that’ is given to the Seer to end that sorrow.”

Verse I.12.6

I.12.6

दृशेश् छाया यदारूढा मुखच्छायेव दर्शने । पश्यंस् तं प्रत्ययं योगी दृष्ट अत्मेति मन्यते

“When the shadow of the Seer has risen in the vision, like the reflection of a face in a mirror, the yogin sees that cognition and thinks, ‘I have seen the Self.’”

Verse I.12.7

I.12.7

तं च मूढं च यद्य् अन्यं प्रत्ययं वेत्ति नो दृशेः । स एव योगिनां श्रेष्ठो नेतरः स्यान् न संशयः

“If one knows that cognition as distinct from the deluded person and from the Seer, that one is the best of yogins; no other is so.”

Verse I.12.8

I.12.8

विज्ञातेर् यस् तु विज्ञाता स त्वम् इत्य् उच्यते यतः । स स्याद् अनुभवस् तस्य ततो ऽन्यो ऽनुभवो मृषा

“Since the knower of awareness itself is called ‘you,’ that is the person’s own experience; any other experience is false.”

Verse I.12.9

I.12.9

दृशिरूपे सदा नित्ये दर्शनादर्शने मयि । कथं स्यातां ततो नान्य इष्यते ऽनुभवस् ततः

“In me, the eternal nature of seeing, how could seeing and not seeing occur? Therefore no other experience is admitted.”

Verse I.12.10

I.12.10

यत्स्थस् तापो रवेर् देहे दृशेः स विषयो यथा । सत्त्वस्थस् तद्वद् एवेह दृशेः स विषयस् तथा

“Just as the heat located in the sun’s body is an object of sight, so what is established in the quality of purity is an object for the Seer.”

Verse I.12.11

I.12.11

प्रतिषिद्धेदमंशो ज्ञः खम् इवैकरसो ऽद्वयः । नित्यमुक्तस् तथा शुद्धः सो ऽहं ब्रह्मास्मि केवलः

“The wise one, having rejected the ‘this’ portion, is one in essence like space, nondual, ever-free and pure. I alone am that Brahman.”

Verse I.12.12

I.12.12

विज्ञातुर् नैव विज्ञाता परो ऽन्यः संभवत्य् अतः । विज्ञाताहं परो मुक्तः सर्वभूतेषु सर्वदा

“There can be no other knower of the knower. Therefore I am the supreme knower, free always and in all beings.”

Verse I.12.13

I.12.13

यो वेदालुप्तदृष्टित्वम् आत्मनो ऽकर्तृतां तथा । ब्रह्मवित्त्वं तथा मुक्त्वा स आत्मज्ञो न चेतरः

“One who knows the Self’s seeing as undiminished, its non-agency, its being Brahman and its freedom is a knower of the Self; no other is.”

Verse I.12.14

I.12.14

ज्ञातैवाहम् अविज्ञेयः शुद्धो मुक्तः सदेत्य् अपि । विवेकी प्रत्ययो बुद्धेर् दृश्यत्वान् नाशवत्त्वतः

“‘I alone am the knower, unknowable, pure and ever free’—this discernment is a thought in the intellect, because the intellect is seen and perishable.”

Verse I.12.15

I.12.15

अलुप्ता त्व् आत्मनो दृष्टिर् नोत्पाद्या कारकैर् यतः । दृश्यया चान्यया दृष्ट्या जन्यतास्याः प्रकल्पिता

“The Self’s seeing is never diminished and need not be produced by factors of action; its being produced by another, visible act of seeing is only imagined.”

Verse I.12.16

I.12.16

देहात्मबुद्ध्यपेक्षत्वाद् आत्मनः कर्तृता मृषा । नैव किंचित् करोमीति सत्या बुद्धिः प्रमाणजा

“The Self’s agency is false because it depends on identifying with the body. The valid cognition ‘I do nothing’ is true.”

Verse I.12.17

I.12.17

कर्तृत्वं कारकापेक्षम् अकर्तृत्वं स्वभावतः । कर्ता भोक्तेति विज्ञानं मृषैवेति सुनिश्चितम्

“Agency depends on the factors of action; non-agency is natural. The thought ‘I am the doer and enjoyer’ is certainly false.”

Verse I.12.18

I.12.18

एवं शास्त्रानुमानाभ्यां स्वरूपे ऽवगते सति । नियोज्यो ऽहम् इति ह्य् एषा सत्या बुद्धिः कथं भवेत्

“When one’s true nature has been understood through scripture and reasoning, how could the thought ‘I am one who must act’ be true?”

Verse I.12.19

I.12.19

यथा सर्वान्तरं व्योम व्योम्नो ऽप्य् अभ्यन्तरो ह्य् अहम् । निर्विकारो ऽचलः शुद्धो ऽजरो मुक्तः सदाद्वयः

“As space is within all things, I am within even space: changeless, unmoving, pure, ageless, free and ever nondual.”

Padyabhāga · Chapter 13: Acakṣuṣṭva-prakaraṇa

Verse I.13.1

I.13.1

अचक्षुष्ट्वान् न दृष्टिर् मे तथाश्रोत्रस्य का श्रुतिः । अवाक्त्वान् न तु वक्तिः स्याद् अमनस्त्वान् मतिः कुतः

“I have no sight, since I have no eyes; what hearing could there be without ears? Since I have no speech, I do not speak; without a mind, how could there be thought?”

Verse I.13.2

I.13.2

अप्राणस्य न कर्मास्ति बुद्ध्यभावे न वेदिता । विद्याविद्ये ततो न स्तश् चिन्मात्रज्योतिषो मम

“Without breath, I have no action; without intellect, no knower. For me, whose light is pure consciousness alone, there is neither knowledge nor ignorance.”

Verse I.13.3

I.13.3

नित्यमुक्तस्य शुद्धस्य कूटस्थस्याविचालिनः । अमृतस्याक्षरस्यैवम् अशरीरस्य सर्वदा

“Thus I am ever-free, pure, changeless, unmoving, immortal, imperishable and always bodiless.”

Verse I.13.4

I.13.4

जिघत्सा वा पिपासा वा शोकमोहौ जरामृती । न विद्यन्ते ऽशरीरत्वाद् व्योमवद् व्यापिनो मम

“Hunger and thirst, grief and delusion, old age and death do not exist for me, who am bodiless and all-pervading like space.”

Verse I.13.5

I.13.5

अस्पर्शत्वान् न मे स्पृष्टिर् नाजिह्वत्वाद् रसज्ञता । नित्यविज्ञानरूपस्य ज्ञानाज्ञाने न मे सदा

“Since I cannot be touched, I have no sensation of touch; having no tongue, I do not taste. I am eternally the nature of awareness, so knowledge and ignorance are never mine.”

Verse I.13.6

I.13.6

या तु स्यान् मानसी वृत्तिश् चाक्षुष्का रूपरञ्जना । नित्यम् एवात्मनो दृष्ट्या नित्यया दृश्यते हि सा

“The mental thought that colors itself with visible form is always seen by the Self’s eternal vision.”

Verse I.13.7

I.13.7

तथान्येन्द्रिययुक्ता या वृत्तयो विषयाञ्जनाः । स्मृती रागादिरूप च केवलान्तर् मनस्य् अपि

“Likewise thoughts joined with other senses and colored by their objects, and memory, attachment and the rest, even when they occur only within the mind—”

Verse I.13.8

I.13.8

मानस्यस् तद्वद् अन्यस्य दृश्यन्ते स्वप्नवृत्तयः । द्रष्टुर् दृष्टिस् ततो नित्या शुद्धानन्ता च केवला

“—are seen like dream-thoughts in another. The Seer’s vision is therefore eternal, pure, infinite and alone.”

Verse I.13.9

I.13.9

अनित्या साविशुद्धेति गृह्यते ऽत्राविवेकतः । सुखी दुःखी तथा चाहं दृश्ययोपाधिभूतया

“Through lack of discrimination it is mistaken for impermanent and impure; the whole world thinks ‘I am happy’ or ‘I am unhappy’ because of the seen limiting adjunct.”

Verse I.13.10

I.13.10

मूढया मूढ इत्य् एवं शुद्धया शुद्ध इत्य् अपि । मन्यते सर्वलोको ऽयं येन संसारम् ऋच्छति

“Deluded by a confused understanding, the world thinks itself deluded or pure, and so enters worldly existence.”

Verse I.13.11

I.13.11

अचक्षुष्ट्वादिशास्त्रोक्तं सबाह्याभ्यन्तरं ह्य् अजम् । नित्यमुक्तम् इहात्मानं मुमुक्षुश् चेत् सदा स्मरेत्

“If a seeker would always remember the Self, unborn inside and out, as taught by scripture through ‘without eyes’ and the rest, one is eternally free.”

Verse I.13.12

I.13.12

अचक्षुष्ट्वादिशास्त्राच् च नेन्द्रियाणि सदा मम । अप्राणो ह्य् अमनाः शुभ्र इति चाथर्वणे वचः

“Scripture says I never have the sense organs, as taught by ‘without eyes’ and the rest; the Atharvan text says, ‘without breath, without mind, pure.’”

Verse I.13.13

I.13.13

शब्दादीनाम् अभावश् च श्रूयते मम काठके । अप्राणो ह्य् अमना यस्माद् अविकारी सदा ह्य् अहम्

“The Kaṭha text also declares that sound and the rest do not belong to me. Since I am without breath or mind, I am always changeless.”

Verse I.13.14

I.13.14

विक्षेपो नास्ति तस्मान् मे न समाधिस् ततो मम । विक्षेपो वा समाधिर् वा मनसः स्याद् विकारिणः

“Therefore distraction does not exist for me, nor does concentration. Distraction and concentration are states of a changing mind.”

Verse I.13.15

I.13.15

अमनस्कस्य शुद्धस्य कथं तत् स्याद् द्वयं मम । अमनस्त्वाविकारित्वे विदेहव्यापिनो मम

“How could either state belong to me, who am mindless, pure and changeless, bodiless and all-pervading?”

Verse I.13.16

I.13.16

इत्य् एतद् यावद् अज्ञानं तावत् कार्यं ममाभवत् । नित्यमुक्तस्य शुद्धस्य बुद्धस्य च सदा मम

“So long as ignorance remained, all this appeared to be my concern. But I am always free, pure and awakened.”

Verse I.13.17

I.13.17

समाधिर् वासमाधिर् वा कार्यं वान्यत् कुतो भवेत् । मं हि ध्यात्वा च बुद्ध्वा च मन्यन्ते कृतकृत्यताम्

“How could concentration, lack of concentration or any other task belong to me? People think they have accomplished their purpose by meditating on me and knowing me.”

Verse I.13.18

I.13.18

अहं ब्रह्मास्मि सर्वो ऽस्मि शुद्धो बुद्धो ऽसितः सदा । अजः सर्वग एवाहम् अजरश् चामृतो ऽक्षयः

“I am Brahman; I am all—pure, awakened, ever unstained. I am unborn, all-pervading, ageless, immortal and imperishable.”

Verse I.13.19

I.13.19

मदन्यः सर्वभूतेषु बोद्धा कश्चिन् न विद्यते । कर्माध्यक्षश् च साक्षी च चेता नित्यो ऽगुणो ऽद्वयः

“There is no knower in all beings other than me. I am the overseer of action, the witness, conscious, eternal, without qualities and nondual.”

Verse I.13.20

I.13.20

न सच् चाहं न चासच् च नोभयं केवलः शिवः । न मे संध्या न रात्रिर् वा नाहर् वा सर्वदा दृशेः

“I am neither existent nor nonexistent, nor both; I am alone, auspicious. For my vision there is no twilight or night, no day, ever.”

Verse I.13.21

I.13.21

सर्वमूर्तिवियुक्तं यद् यथा खं सूक्ष्मम् अद्वयम् । तेनाप्य् अस्मि विनाभूतं ब्रह्मैवाहं तथाद्वयम्

“As space is subtle, nondual and free of every form, so too I am Brahman, untouched by anything, nondual.”

Verse I.13.22

I.13.22

ममात्मास्य त आत्मेति भेदो व्योम्नो यथा भवेत् । एकस्य सुषिभेदेन तथा मम विकल्पितः

“The distinctions ‘my Self’ and ‘your Self’ are imagined in me, as divisions of space are imagined through the openings in a single space.”

Verse I.13.23

I.13.23

भेदो ऽभेदस् तथा चैको नाना चेति विकल्पितम् । ज्ञेयं ज्ञाता गतिर् गन्ता मय्य् एकस्मिन् कुतो भवेत्

“Difference and nondifference, oneness and multiplicity are all imagined. How could the known, the knower, the path and the traveler exist in me alone?”

Verse I.13.24

I.13.24

न मे हेयं न चादेयम् अविकारी यतो ह्य् अहम् । सदा मुक्तस् तथा शुद्धः सदा बुद्धो ऽगुणो ऽद्वयः

“Nothing is to be given up or taken up by me, for I am changeless, ever-free, pure, awakened, without qualities and nondual.”

Verse I.13.25

I.13.25

इत्य् एवं सर्वदात्मानं विद्यात् सर्वं समाहितः । विदित्वा मां स्वदेहस्थम् ऋषिर् मुक्तो ध्रुवो भवेत्

“With a collected mind, know the Self in this way at all times. Knowing me, present in one’s own body, the sage surely becomes free.”

Verse I.13.26

I.13.26

कृतकृत्यश् च सिद्धश् च योगी ब्राह्मण एव च । यदैवं वेद तत्त्वार्थम् अन्यथा ह्य् आत्महा भवेत्

“When one knows the truth in this way, one is accomplished, fulfilled, a yogin and a true knower of Brahman; otherwise one destroys one’s own Self.”

Verse I.13.27

I.13.27

वेदार्थो निश्चितो ह्य् एष समासेन मयोदितः । संन्यासिभ्यः प्रवक्तव्यः शान्तेभ्यः शिष्टबुद्धिना

“In brief, I have stated this certain meaning of the Veda. It should be taught to renunciants who are peaceful and wise.”

Padyabhāga · Chapter 14: Svapnasmṛti-prakaraṇa

Verse I.14.1

I.14.1

स्वप्नस्मृत्योर् घटादेर् हि रूपाभासः प्रदृश्यते । पुरा नूनं तदाकरा धीर् दृष्टेत्य् अनुमीयते

“In a dream and in memory, the form of a pot and the like is seen; one infers that earlier there must have been a cognition shaped like it.”

Verse I.14.2

I.14.2

भिक्षाम् अटन् यथा स्वप्ने दृष्टो देहो न स स्वयम् । जाग्रद्दृश्यात् तथा देहाद् द्रष्टृत्वाद् अन्य एव सः

“The body seen in a dream while begging is not the dreamer himself; likewise, because the waking body is seen, the Seer is other than it.”

Verse I.14.3

I.14.3

मूषासिक्तं यथा ताम्रं तन्निभं जायते तथा । रूपादीन् व्याप्नुवच् चित्तं तन्निभं दृश्यते ध्रुवम्

“As copper poured into a mold takes its form, so the mind, pervading visible forms and the rest, surely appears to take their shape.”

Verse I.14.4

I.14.4

व्यञ्जको वा यथालोको व्यङ्ग्यस्याकारताम् इयात् । सर्वार्थव्यञ्जकत्वाद् धीर् अर्थाकरा प्रदृश्यते

“As light takes the form of what it reveals, the intellect appears shaped like its objects because it reveals them all.”

Verse I.14.5

I.14.5

धिर् एवार्थस्वरूपा हि पुंसा दृष्टा पुरापि च । न चेत् स्वप्ने कथं पश्येत् स्मरतो वाकृतिः कुतः

“The intellect has already been seen to take the form of its object. If not, how would one see in a dream, and how could memory have a form?”

Verse I.14.6

I.14.6

व्यञ्जकत्वं तद् एवास्य रूपाद्याकारदृश्यता । द्रष्टृत्वं च दृशेस् तद्वद् व्याप्तिः स्याद् धिय उद्भवे

“Its revealing power is just this appearance as the forms of color and the rest. The Seer’s vision likewise pervades the intellect as it arises.”

Verse I.14.7

I.14.7

चिन्मात्रज्योतिषा सर्वः सर्वदेहेषु बुद्धयः । मया यस्मात् प्रकाश्यन्ते सर्वस्यात्मा ततो ह्य् अहम्

“Because I illumine the intellects in every body by the light of pure consciousness, I am therefore the Self of all.”

Verse I.14.8

I.14.8

करणं कर्म कर्ता च क्रिया स्वप्ने फलं च धीः । जाग्रत्य् एवं यतो दृष्टा द्रष्टा तस्मात् ततो ऽन्यथा

“In a dream, the instrument, action, agent and result are all seen as intellect; the waking world is seen in the same way. The Seer is different from them.”

Verse I.14.9

I.14.9

बुद्ध्यादीनाम् अनात्मत्वं हेयोपादेयरूपतः । हानोपादानकर्तात्मा न त्याज्यो न च गृह्यते

“The intellect and the rest are not-Self, to be relinquished or taken up; the Self is the one who relinquishes and takes up, but is itself neither abandoned nor grasped.”

Verse I.14.10

I.14.10

सबाह्याभ्यन्तरे शुद्धे प्रज्ञानैकरसे घने । बाह्यम् आभ्यन्तरं चान्यत् कथं हेयं प्रकल्प्यते

“Within and without, all is a single mass of pure awareness; how could anything outside or inside be imagined as separate and rejected?”

Verse I.14.11

I.14.11

य आत्मा नेति नेतीति परापोहेन शेषितः । स चेद् ब्रह्मविदात्मेष्टो यतेतातः परं कथम्

“If the Self, retained after every other thing is excluded, is held to be Brahman by the knower, what further effort could be required?”

Verse I.14.12

I.14.12

अशनायाद्यतिक्रान्तं ब्रह्मैवास्मि निरन्तरम् । कार्यवान् स्यां कथं चाहं विमृशेद् एवम् अञ्जसा

“I am Brahman, without interruption, beyond hunger and the rest. How could I, in truth, think that I have any task to do?”

Verse I.14.13

I.14.13

पारगस् तु यथा नद्यास् तत्स्थः परं यियासति । आत्मज्ञश् चेत् तथा कार्यं कर्तुम् अन्यद् इहेच्छति

“Just as one who has crossed to the far bank of a river would not wish to do some other work there, so too a knower of the Self has no further task to seek here.”

Verse I.14.14

I.14.14

आत्मज्ञस्यापि यस्य स्याद् धानोपादानता यदि । न मोक्षार्हः स विज्ञेयो वान्तो ऽसौ ब्रह्मणा ध्रुवम्

“If a knower of the Self still accepts and rejects things, know that person is not fit for liberation; that person has surely been overcome by the world of Brahman.”

Verse I.14.15

I.14.15

सादित्यं हि जगत् प्राणस् तस्मान् नाहर् निशैव वा । प्राणज्ञस्यापि न स्यातां कुतो ब्रह्मविदो ऽद्वये

“The world, with its sun and the rest, is life-breath itself; for the knower of breath there is neither day nor night. How much less could these exist for the knower of nondual Brahman?”

Verse I.14.16

I.14.16

न स्मरत्य् आत्मनो ह्य् आत्मा विस्मरेद् वाप्य् अलुप्तचित् । मनो ऽपि स्मरतीत्य् एतज् ज्ञानम् अज्ञानहेतुजम्

“The Self does not remember itself, nor does it forget itself, for awareness is never lost. The mind remembers; this knowledge and ignorance arise from a cause.”

Verse I.14.17

I.14.17

ज्ञातुर् ज्ञेयः परो ह्य् आत्मा सो ऽविद्याकल्पितः स्मृतः । अपोढे विद्यया तस्मिन् रज्ज्वां सर्प इवाद्वयः

“A second Self, beyond the knower, is imagined through ignorance. When it is removed by knowledge, the Self is nondual, like the rope mistaken for a snake.”

Verse I.14.18

I.14.18

कर्तृकर्मफलाभावात् सबाह्याभ्यन्तरं ह्य् अजम् । ममाहं चेति यो भावस् तस्मिन् कस्य कुतो भवेत्

“Since there is no agent, action or result, and the Self is unborn within and without, for whom and how could the thought ‘I’ or ‘mine’ arise?”

Verse I.14.19

I.14.19

आत्मा ह्य् आत्मीय इत्य् एष भावो ऽविद्याप्रकल्पितः । आत्मैकत्वे ह्य् असौ नास्ति बीजभावे कुतः फलम्

“The notion ‘the Self is mine’ is imagined through ignorance. When the Self is one, it does not exist; without a seed, how could there be a fruit?”

Verse I.14.20

I.14.20

द्रष्टृ स्रोतृ तथा मन्तृ विज्ञातृ एव तद् अक्षरम् । द्रष्ट्राद्यन्यन् न तद् यस्मात् तस्माद् द्रष्टाहम् अक्षरम्

“The one who sees, hears, thinks and knows is the Imperishable. Since no one is other than the Seer and the rest, I am that Seer, the Imperishable.”

Verse I.14.21

I.14.21

स्थावरं जङ्गमं चैव द्रष्टृत्वादिक्रियायुतम् । सर्वम् अक्षरम् एवातः सर्वस्यात्माक्षरं त्व् अहम्

“All beings, moving and unmoving, endowed with acts such as seeing, are the Imperishable; therefore I am the Imperishable Self of all.”

Verse I.14.22

I.14.22

अकार्यशेषम् आत्मानम् अक्रियात्मक्रियाफलम् । निर्ममं निरहंकारं यः पश्यति स पश्यति

“One who sees the Self as free of every object and action, with no remainder of agency or result, and without ‘mine’ or ego, truly sees.”

Verse I.14.23

I.14.23

ममाहंकारयत्नेच्छाः शून्या एव स्वभावतः । आत्मनीति यदि ज्ञातम् आध्वं स्वस्थाः किम् ईहितैः

“The thoughts ‘mine’ and ‘I,’ effort and desire, are empty by nature. If this is known to be in the Self, why strive? Be at peace.”

Verse I.14.24

I.14.24

यो ऽहंकर्तारम् आत्मानं तथा वेत्तारम् एव यः । वेत्त्य् अनात्मज्ञ एवासौ यो ऽन्यथाज्ञः स आत्मवित्

“Whoever knows the Self as the doer, and also as the knower, is not a knower of the Self; the one who knows it otherwise is the knower.”

Verse I.14.25

I.14.25

यथान्यत्वे ऽपि तादात्म्यं देहादिष्व् आत्मनो मतम् । तथाकर्तुर् अविज्ञानात् फलकर्मात्मतात्मनः

“As the Self is thought to be identical with the body and the rest, though distinct from them, so through ignorance the Self is identified with agency, knowledge and the fruits of action.”

Verse I.14.26

I.14.26

दृष्टिः श्रुतिर् मतिर् ज्ञातिः स्वप्ने दृष्टा जनैः सदा । तासाम् आत्मस्वरूपत्वाद् अतः प्रत्यक्षतात्मनः

“Seeing, hearing, thinking and knowing are experienced by people in dreams; since they belong to the Self, the Self is directly evident.”

Verse I.14.27

I.14.27

परलोकभयं यस्य नास्ति मृत्युभयं तथा । तस्यात्मज्ञस्य शोच्यः स्युः सब्रह्मेन्द्रा अपीश्वराः

“For the knower of the Self, who fears neither the next world nor death, even the gods, including Brahmā and Indra, are objects of pity.”

Verse I.14.28

I.14.28

ईश्वरत्वेन किं तस्य ब्रह्मेन्द्रत्वेन वा पुनः । तृष्णा चेत् सर्वतश् छिन्ना सर्वदैन्योद्भवाशुभा

“What need has that person of lordship or the rank of Indra, when craving is cut off everywhere, along with the source of all misery?”

Verse I.14.29

I.14.29

अहम् इत्य् आत्मधीर् या च ममेत्य् आत्मीयधीर् अपि । अर्थशून्ये यदा यस्य स आत्मज्ञो भवेत् तदा

“When a person’s notions ‘I’ and ‘mine’ have no object at all, that person is then a knower of the Self.”

Verse I.14.30

I.14.30

बुद्ध्यादौ सत्य् उपाधौ च तथासत्य् अविशेषता । यस्य चेद् आत्मनो ज्ञाता तस्य कार्यं कथं भवेत्

“When the intellect and its limiting adjunct are present, or when they are absent, the knower of the Self remains without distinction. How could any task belong to that one?”

Verse I.14.31

I.14.31

प्रसन्ने विमले व्योम्नि प्रज्ञानैकरसे ऽद्वये । उत्पन्नात्मधियो ब्रूत किम् अन्यत् कार्यम् इष्यते

“Tell me what further task is desired when knowledge of the Self has arisen in the clear, stainless, nondual sky of pure consciousness.”

Verse I.14.32

I.14.32

आत्मानं सर्वभूतस्थम् अमित्रं चात्मनो ऽपि यः । पश्यन्न् इच्छत्य् असौ नूनं शीतीकर्तुं विभावसुम्

“One who sees the Self dwelling in all beings, and even as an enemy to oneself, surely wishes to cool the blazing fire.”

Verse I.14.33

I.14.33

प्रज्ञाप्राणानुकार्य् आत्मा छायेवाक्षादिगोचरः । ध्यायतीवेति चोक्तो हि शुद्धो मुक्तः स्वतो हि सः

“The Self appears to follow the intellect and life-breath, like a shadow appearing within the range of the eye; scripture says it ‘seems to think.’ Yet by nature it is pure and free.”

Verse I.14.34

I.14.34

अप्राणस्यामनस्कस्य तथासंसर्गिणो दृशेः । व्योमवद् व्यापिनो ह्य् अस्य कथं कार्यं भवेन् मम

“For me, the Seer without breath or mind, unattached and all-pervading like space, how could there be any task?”

Verse I.14.35

I.14.35

असमाधिं न पश्यामि निर्विकारस्य सर्वदा । ब्रह्मणो मे विशुद्धस्य शोध्यं चान्यद् विपाप्मनः

“For me, the changeless, ever-pure Brahman, I see no lack of concentration or anything else that needs purification, since I am free from evil.”

Verse I.14.36

I.14.36

गन्तव्यं च तथैवाहं सर्वगस्याचलस्य च । नोर्ध्वं नाधस् तिरो वापि निष्कलस्यागुणत्वतः

“I am indeed what is to be reached, all-pervading and unmoving; I am neither above, below nor across, because I am without parts or qualities.”

Verse I.14.37

I.14.37

चिन्मात्रज्योतिषो नित्यं तमस् तस्मिन् न विद्यते । कथं कार्यं ममैवाद्य नित्यमुक्तस्य शिष्यते

“There is no darkness for me, whose light is pure consciousness alone. What task could remain for me, eternally free even now?”

Verse I.14.38

I.14.38

अमनस्कस्य का चिन्ता क्रिया वानिन्द्रियस्य का । अप्राणो ह्य् अमनाः शुभ्र इति सत्यं श्रुतेर् वचः

“What thought can there be for one without a mind? What action for one without senses? Scripture truly says: ‘without breath, without mind, pure.’”

Verse I.14.39

I.14.39

अकालत्वाद् अदेशत्वाद् अदिक्त्वाद् अनिमित्ततः । आत्मनो नैव कालादेर् अपेक्षा ध्यायतः सदा

“Because the Self is beyond time, place, direction and cause, it has no need of time or the rest, even for one who meditates on it always.”

Verse I.14.40

I.14.40

यस्मिन् देवाश् च वेदाश् च पवित्रं कृत्स्नम् एकताम् । व्रजेत् तन् मानसं तीर्थं यस्मिन् स्नात्वामृतो भवेत्

“That in which the gods, the Vedas and all that is pure attain unity is the sacred ford of the mind; bathing in it, one becomes immortal.”

Verse I.14.41

I.14.41

न चास्ति शब्दादिर् अनन्यवेदनः परस्परेणापि न चैव दृश्यते । परेण दृश्यास् तु यथा रसादयस् तथैव दृश्यत्वत एव दैहिकाः

“Sound and the rest do not know one another, nor do they appear to one another; they are seen by another, as taste and the rest are. The bodily senses and their objects are likewise seen.”

Verse I.14.42

I.14.42

अहं ममेत्य् एषणयत्नविक्रियाः सुखादयस् तद्वद् इह प्रदृश्यतः । दृश्यत्वयोगाच् च परस्परेण ते न दृश्यतां यान्ति ततः परो भवेत्

“The thoughts ‘I’ and ‘mine,’ desire, effort, change and happiness are all seen here; because they are seen, they do not see one another. The Seer is beyond them.”

Verse I.14.43

I.14.43

अहंक्रियाद्या हि समस्तविक्रिया सकर्तृका कर्मफलेन संहिता । चितिस्वरूपेण समन्ततो ऽर्कवत् प्रकाश्यमानासिततात्मनो ह्य् अतः

“All changes, beginning with ego, are joined to an agent, action and result, and are illuminated on every side by the light of consciousness; therefore the Self is pure and beyond them.”

Verse I.14.44

I.14.44

दृशिस्वरूपेण हि सर्वदेहिनां वियद् यथा व्याप्य मनांस्य् अवस्थितः । अतो न तस्माद् अपरो ऽस्ति वेदिता परो ऽपि तस्माद् अत एक ईश्वरः

“As space pervades the minds of all embodied beings, the Seer abides in all as their seeing nature. There is no knower apart from it; the supreme Lord is therefore one.”

Verse I.14.45

I.14.45

शरीरबुद्ध्योर् यदि चान्यदृश्यता निरात्मवादाः सुनिराकृटा मया । परश् च सिद्धो ह्य् अविशुद्धिकर्मतः सुनिर्मलः सर्वगतो ऽसितो ऽद्वयः

“If the body and intellect are seen as other, I have fully refuted the doctrines that deny the Self. The supreme Self is established as pure, all-pervading, unstained and nondual.”

Verse I.14.46

I.14.46

घटादिरूपं यदि ते न गृह्यते मनः प्रवृत्तं बहुधा स्ववृत्तिभिः । अशुद्ध्यचिद्रूपविकारदोषता मतेर् यथा वारयितुं न पार्यते

“Even if you do not grasp the forms of pots and the rest, the mind is active in its many thoughts; its defects of impurity, insentience and change cannot thereby be prevented.”

Verse I.14.47

I.14.47

यथा विशुद्धं गगनं निरन्तरं न सज्जते नापि च लिप्यते तथा । समस्तभूतेषु सदैव तेष्व् अयं समः सदात्मा ह्य् अजरो ऽमरो ऽभयः

“As the stainless, unbroken sky neither clings nor is stained, so this eternal Self is always equal in all beings, ageless, deathless and fearless.”

Verse I.14.48

I.14.48

अमूर्तमूर्तानि च कर्मवासना दृशिस्वरूपस्य बहिः प्रकल्पिताः । अविद्यया ह्य् आत्मनि मूढदृष्टिभिर् अपोह्य नेतीत्य् अवशेषितो दृशिः

“Immaterial and material forms, together with the latent impressions of action, are imagined outside the nature of the Seer. Through ignorance the deluded project them on the Self; when they are set aside as ‘not this,’ the Seer alone remains.”

Verse I.14.49

I.14.49

प्रबोधरूपं मनसो ऽर्थयोगजं स्मृतौ च सुप्तस्य च दृश्यते ऽर्थवत् । तथैव देहप्रतिमानतः पृथग् दृशेः शरीरं च मनश् च दृश्यतः

“The mind’s waking awareness, arising from contact with an object, is seen; the sleeping person’s memory is seen too, as are the body and mind, distinct from the Seer.”

Verse I.14.50

I.14.50

स्वभावशुद्धे गगने घनादिके मले ऽपयाते सति चाविशेषता । यथा च तद्वच् छ्रुतिवारितद्वये सदाविशेषो गगनोपमे दृशौ

“When clouds and other stains depart, the naturally pure sky has no division. Likewise, once scripture has removed duality, the Seer remains ever undivided, like space.”

Padyabhāga · Chapter 15: Nānyad-anyat-prakaraṇa

Verse I.15.1

I.15.1

नान्यद् अन्यद् भवेद् यस्मान् नान्यत् किंचिद् विचिन्तयेत् । अन्यस्यान्यत्वभावे हि नाशस् तस्य ध्रुवो भवेत्

“Nothing becomes something other than itself; one should therefore not imagine anything as other. If one thing truly became another, it would surely perish.”

Verse I.15.2

I.15.2

स्मरतो दृश्यते दृष्टं पटे चित्रम् इवार्पितम् । यत्र येन च तौ ज्ञेयौ सत्त्वक्षेत्रज्ञसंज्ञकौ

“What is remembered appears as an image painted on a cloth. In that, both the existent and the knower of the field are to be understood—where and by what are they known?”

Verse I.15.3

I.15.3

फलान्तं चानुभूतं यद् युक्तं कर्त्रादिकारकैः । स्मर्यमाणं हि कर्मस्थं पूर्वं कर्मैव तत् ततः

“What has been experienced through agent, action and other factors, up to its result, is remembered as belonging to action; that, then, is the earlier action itself.”

Verse I.15.4

I.15.4

द्रष्टुश् चान्यद् भवेद् दृश्यं दृश्यत्वाद् घटवत् सदा । दृश्याद् द्रष्टासजातीयो न धीवत् साक्षितान्यथा

“The seen is always different from the Seer, like a pot. The Seer is of a different kind from what is seen, unlike the intellect; otherwise there could be no witnessing.”

Verse I.15.5

I.15.5

स्वात्मबुद्धिम् अपेक्ष्यासौ विधीनां स्यात् प्रयोजकः । जात्यादिः शववत् तेन तद्वन् नानात्मतान्यथा

“If the Seer depended on self-knowledge as an object, ritual injunctions could apply to it; but, like a corpse, it has no such qualities. Therefore it cannot be not-Self.”

Verse I.15.6

I.15.6

न प्रियाप्रिय इत्य् उक्तेर् नादेहत्वं क्रियाफलम् । देहयोगः क्रियाहेतुस् तस्माद् विद्वान् क्रियास् त्यजेत्

“The statement that pleasure and pain do not belong to the Self does not mean that bodilessness is a result of action. Embodiment is the cause of action; therefore the wise should relinquish actions.”

Verse I.15.7

I.15.7

कर्मस्व् आत्मा स्वतन्त्रश् चेन् निवृत्तौ च तथेष्यताम् । अदेहत्वे फले ऽकार्ये ज्ञाते कुर्यात् कथं क्रियाः

“If the Self is independent with respect to action, it should be so with respect to cessation as well. When the result is known to be bodilessness, how could one perform action?”

Verse I.15.8

I.15.8

जात्यादिन् संपरित्यज्य निमित्तं कर्मणां बुधः । कर्महेतुविरुद्धं यत् स्वरूपं शास्त्रतः स्मरेत्

“Having set aside birth, caste and the rest, the wise should remember through scripture the Self’s nature, which is contrary to the cause of action.”

Verse I.15.9

I.15.9

आत्मैकः सर्वभूतेषु तानि तस्मिंश् च खे यथा । पर्यगाद् व्योमवत् सर्वं शुक्रं दीप्तिमद् इष्यते

“The Self is one in all beings and pervades them, as space pervades all. It is pure and radiant; I am that.”

Verse I.15.10

I.15.10

व्रणस्नाय्वोर् अभावेन स्थूलं देहं निवारयेत् । शुद्धापापतया लेपं लिङ्गं चाकायम् इत्य् उत

“One should set aside the gross body, which has wounds, tendons and the rest; its stain does not touch the pure and sinless Self, which is subtle and bodiless.”

Verse I.15.11

I.15.11

वासुदेवो यथाश्वत्थे स्वदेहे चाब्रवित् समम् । तद्वद् वेत्ति य आत्मानं समं स ब्रह्मवित्तमः

“As Vāsudeva declared himself equal in the aśvattha tree and in his own body, so one who knows the Self equally in all is the highest knower of Brahman.”

Verse I.15.12

I.15.12

यथा ह्य् अन्यशरीरेषु ममाहन्ता न चेष्यते । अस्मिंश् चापि तथा देहे धीसाक्षित्वाविशेषतः

“Just as I do not identify with other bodies, neither should I identify with this body, since I equally witness the intellect in all.”

Verse I.15.13

I.15.13

रूपसंस्कारतुल्याधि रागद्वेषौ भयं च यत् । गृह्यते धीश्रयं तस्माज् ज्ञाता शुद्धो ऽभयः सदा

“Color and impressions, attachment, aversion and fear are grasped as belonging to the intellect; therefore the knower is always pure and fearless.”

Verse I.15.14

I.15.14

यन्मनास् तन्मयो ऽन्यत्वे नात्मत्वाप्तौ क्रियात्मनि । आत्मत्वे चानपेक्षत्वात् सापेक्षं हि न तत् स्वयम्

“The mind becomes what it attends to, but that does not make it the Self; reaching the Self is not an action. Since the Self is always itself, it depends on nothing.”

Verse I.15.15

I.15.15

खम् इवैकरसा ज्ञप्तिर् अविभक्ताजरामला । चक्षुराद्युपधानात् सा विपरीता विभाव्यते

“Awareness is undivided and of one nature, like space, ageless and unstained; the sense organs as limiting adjuncts make it appear otherwise.”

Verse I.15.16

I.15.16

दृश्यत्वाद् अहम् इत्य् एष नात्मधर्मो घटादिवत् । तथान्ये प्रत्यया ज्ञेया दोषाश् चात्मामलो ह्य् अतः

“Because it is seen, the thought ‘I’ is not a property of the Self, like a pot; the other thoughts are likewise known, and their defects do not stain the Self.”

Verse I.15.17

I.15.17

सर्वप्रत्ययसाक्षित्वाद् अविकारी च सर्वगः । विक्रियेत यदि द्रष्टा बुद्ध्यादीवाल्पविद् भवेत्

“Because the Self witnesses every thought, it is changeless and all-pervading. If the Seer changed, it would become a limited knower like the intellect.”

Verse I.15.18

I.15.18

न दृष्टिर् लुप्यते द्रष्टुश् चक्षुरादेर् यथैव तु । नहि द्रष्टुर् इति ह्य् उक्तं तस्माद् द्रष्टा सदैव भुक्

“The Seer’s vision is never destroyed, even as the eye and the other senses are; scripture says the Seer itself is never destroyed, and thus is always the experiencer.”

Verse I.15.19

I.15.19

संघातो वास्मि भूतानां करणानां तथैव च । व्यस्तं वान्यतमो वास्मि को आस्मीति विचारयेत्

“Am I an aggregate of elements and senses, or each of them separately? One should inquire: ‘What am I?’”

Verse I.15.20

I.15.20

व्यस्तं नाहं समस्तं वा भूतम् इन्द्रियम् एव वा । ज्ञेयत्वात् करणत्वाच् च ज्ञातान्यो ऽस्माद् घटादिवत्

“I am neither an individual element nor their aggregate, nor any single sense. Because they are known and serve as instruments, I am distinct from them, like a pot.”

Verse I.15.21

I.15.21

आत्माग्नेर् इन्धना बुद्धिर् अविद्याकामकर्मभिः । दीपिता प्रज्वलत्य् एष द्वारैः श्रोत्रादिभिः सदा

“The intellect is fuel for the fire of the Self. Ignorance, desire and action kindle it; it blazes continually through the openings of hearing and the other senses.”

Verse I.15.22

I.15.22

दक्षिणाक्षिप्रधानेषु यदा बुद्धिर् विचेष्टते । विषयैर् हविषा दीप्ता आत्माग्निः स्थूलभुक् तदा

“When the intellect operates chiefly through the right eye, the fire of the Self, kindled by the offerings of sense objects, then consumes the gross world.”

Verse I.15.23

I.15.23

हूयन्ते तु हवींषीति रूपादिग्रहणे स्मरन् । अरागद्वेष आत्माग्नौ जाग्रद्दोषैर् न लिप्यते

“Remembering that forms and the rest are offered as oblations, the Self-fire, free of attachment and aversion, is not stained by the defects of waking.”

Verse I.15.24

I.15.24

मानसे तु गृहे व्यक्ता अविद्याकर्मवासनाः । पश्यंस् तैजस आत्मोक्तः स्वयंज्योतिःप्रकाशिताः

“When impressions of ignorance and action appear in the house of the mind, the Self is called Taijasa, seeing them illumined by its own light.”

Verse I.15.25

I.15.25

विषया वासना वापि चोद्यन्ते नैव कर्मभिः । यदा बुद्धौ तदा ज्ञेयः प्राज्ञ आत्मा ह्य् अनन्यदृक्

“When objects or impressions are projected in the intellect, the Self is to be known as Prājña, seeing no other.”

Verse I.15.26

I.15.26

मनोबुद्धीन्द्रियाणां या अवस्थाः कर्मचोदिताः । चैतन्येनैव भास्यन्ते रविणेव घटादयः

“The states of mind, intellect and senses, prompted by action, are all illumined by consciousness alone, as pots and the rest are by the sun.”

Verse I.15.27

I.15.27

तत्रैवं सति बुद्धीर् ज्ञ आत्मभासावभासयन् । कर्ता तासां यदर्थास् ता मूढैर् एवाभिधीयते

“The knower, illuminating the intellects by the light of the Self, is called their agent by the deluded, because they are the objects those intellects attend to.”

Verse I.15.28

I.15.28

सर्वज्ञो ऽप्य् अत एव स्यात् स्वेन भासावभासयन् । सर्वं सर्वक्रियाहेतोः सर्वकृत्त्वं तथात्मनः

“For this very reason the Self is all-knowing: illuminating everything by its own light, it is the cause of every action and is called the doer of all.”

Verse I.15.29

I.15.29

सोपाधिश् चैवम् आत्मोक्तो निरुपाख्यो ऽनुपाधिकः । निष्कलो निर्गुणः शुद्धस् तं मनो वाक् च नाप्नुतः

“The Self is described with limiting adjuncts, but without them it is beyond description, partless, qualityless and pure; mind and speech do not reach it.”

Verse I.15.30

I.15.30

चेतनो ऽचेतनो वापि कर्ताकर्ता गतो ऽगतः । बद्धो मुक्तस् तथा चैको नैकः शुद्धो ऽन्यथेति वा

“Conscious or unconscious, agent or non-agent, moving or unmoving, bound or free, one or many, pure or otherwise—these are all [ways it is spoken of].”

Verse I.15.31

I.15.31

अप्राप्यैव निवर्तन्ते वाचो धीभिः सहैव तु । निर्गुणत्वात् क्रियाभावाद् विशेषाणां ह्य् अभावतः

“Words, along with the intellect, turn back without reaching it, because it has no qualities, action or distinctions.”

Verse I.15.32

I.15.32

व्यापकं सर्वतो व्योम मूर्तैः सर्वैर् वियोजितम् । यथा तद्वद् इहात्मानं विद्याच् छुद्धं परं पदम्

“As space is all-pervading and separate from every material form, so should one know the Self here as the pure, supreme reality.”

Verse I.15.33

I.15.33

दृष्टं हित्वा स्मृतिं तस्मिन् सर्वग्रश् च तमस् त्यजेत् । सर्वदृग् ज्योतिषा युक्तो दिनकृच् छार्वरं यथा

“Set aside what is seen, memory and darkness; joined with the light of the all-seeing, be like the sun that dispels the night.”

Verse I.15.34

I.15.34

रूपस्मृत्यन्धकारार्थाः प्रत्यया यस्य गोचराः । स एवात्मा समो द्रष्टा सर्वभूतेषु सर्वगः

“The Self is that which has thoughts of form, memory and darkness as its objects—the equal Seer, all-pervading in all beings.”

Verse I.15.35

I.15.35

आत्मबुद्धिमनश्चक्षुर्विषयालोकसंगमात् । विचित्रो जायते बुद्धेः प्रत्ययो ऽज्ञानलक्षणः

“When the thought of Self, the mind, the eye, objects and light come together, the intellect produces diverse cognitions marked by ignorance.”

Verse I.15.36

I.15.36

विविच्यास्मात् स्वम् आत्मानं विद्याच् छुद्धं परं पदम् । द्रष्टारं सर्वभूतस्थं समं सर्वभयातिगम्

“Discerning one’s true Self from these, know it as the pure, supreme reality, the Seer dwelling in all beings, equal and beyond every fear.”

Verse I.15.37

I.15.37

समस्तं सर्वगं शान्तं विमलं व्योमवत् स्थितम् । निष्कलं निष्क्रियं सर्वं नित्यं द्वन्द्वैर् विवर्जितम्

“It is complete, all-pervading, peaceful, pure and established like space; partless, actionless, all, eternal and free from every pair of opposites.”

Verse I.15.38

I.15.38

सर्वप्रत्ययसाक्षी ज्ञः कथं ज्ञेयो मयेत्य् उत । विमृश्यैवं विजानीयाज् ज्ञातं ब्रह्म न वेति वा

“How could the knower, witness of every cognition, be known by me?” Having examined this, one should discern whether Brahman has been understood or not.

Verse I.15.39

I.15.39

अदृष्टं द्रष्ट्र् अविज्ञातं दभ्रम् इत्यादिशासनात् । नैव ज्ञेयं मयान्यैर् वा परं ब्रह्म कथंचन

“The supreme Brahman cannot in any way be known by me or another, as scripture says: ‘unseen, yet the Seer; unknown, yet the Knower; subtle…’”

Verse I.15.40

I.15.40

स्वरूपाव्यवधानाभ्यां ज्ञानालोकस्वभावतः । अन्यज्ञानानपेक्षत्वाज् ज्ञातं चैव सदा मया

“Because it is not separated from its own nature, because its nature is the light of knowledge, and because it depends on no other cognition, it is always known by me.”

Verse I.15.41

I.15.41

नान्येन ज्योतिषा कार्यं रवेर् आत्मप्रकाशने । स्वबोधान् नान्यबोधेच्छा बोधस्यात्मप्रकाशने

“The sun needs no other light in order to illumine itself; awareness, by its own light, has no wish for another awareness to illumine it.”

Verse I.15.42

I.15.42

न तस्यैवान्यतो ऽपेक्षा स्वरूपं यस्य यद् भवेत् । प्रकाशान्तरदृश्यो न प्रकाशो ह्य् अस्ति कश्चन

“Nothing whose nature is light depends on another to reveal that nature; no light exists that must itself be seen by a different light.”

Verse I.15.43

I.15.43

व्यक्तिः स्याद् अप्रकाशस्य प्रकाशात्मसमागमात् । प्रकाशस् त्व् अर्ककार्यः स्याद् इति मिथ्य वचो ह्य् अतः

“An unmanifest thing becomes manifest when it meets what is luminous; the claim that light is an effect of the sun is therefore false.”

Verse I.15.44

I.15.44

यतो ऽभूत्वा भवेद् यच् च तस्य तत् कार्यम् इष्यते । स्वरूपत्वाद् अभूत्वा न प्रकाशो जायते रवेः

“What was not manifest and becomes manifest is regarded as an effect; but light does not arise from nonexistence, because light is the sun’s very nature.”

Verse I.15.45

I.15.45

सत्तामात्रे प्रकाशस्य कर्तादित्यादिर् इष्यते । घटादिव्यक्तितो यद्वत् तद्वद् बोधात्मनीष्यताम्

“The sun is called the maker only of light’s manifestation, not of its existence; in the same way, awareness is the nature of the Self and is said to manifest things such as pots.”

Verse I.15.46

I.15.46

बिलात् सर्पस्य निर्याणे सूर्यो यद्वत् प्रकाशकः । प्रयत्नेन विना तद्वज् ज्ञातात्मा बोधरूपतः

“As the sun illumines a snake emerging from a hole without effort, so the knower of the Self, by the nature of awareness, illumines without effort.”

Verse I.15.47

I.15.47

दग्धैवम् उष्णः सत्तायां तद्वद् बोधात्मनीष्यताम् । सत्य् एव यद् उपाधौ तु ज्ञाते सर्प इवोत्थिते

“Just as heat is inseparable from fire, awareness is inseparable from the Self. The adjunct may appear as a real snake, but once it is known, it is seen as it is.”

Verse I.15.48

I.15.48

ज्ञातायत्नो ऽपि तद्वज् ज्ञः कर्ता भ्रामकवद् भवेत् । स्वरूपेण स्वयं नात्मा ज्ञेयो ऽज्ञेयो ऽथवा ततः

“Though knowledge seems to require effort, the knower seems to become an agent, as in an illusion. By its own nature the Self is neither an object to be known nor something unknowable.”

Verse I.15.49

I.15.49

विदिताविदिताभ्यां तद् अन्यद् एवेति शासनात् । बन्धमोक्षादयो भावास् तद्वद् आत्मनि कल्पिताः

“Scripture says the Self is other than both the known and the unknown; bondage, liberation and the rest are likewise imagined in it.”

Verse I.15.50

I.15.50

नाहोरात्रे यथा सूर्ये प्रभारूपाविशेषतः । बोधरूपाविशेषान् न बोधाबोधौ तथात्मनि

“As day and night do not belong to the sun, which is simply light, neither awareness nor its absence belongs to the Self, whose nature is awareness.”

Verse I.15.51

I.15.51

यथोक्तं ब्रह्म यो वेद हानोपादानवर्जितम् । यथोक्तेन विधानेन स सत्यं नैव जायते

“One who knows Brahman as taught here, free of taking up and laying down, truly does not undergo birth.”

Verse I.15.52

I.15.52

जन्ममृत्युप्रवाहेषु पतितो नैव शक्नुयात् । इत उद्धर्तुम् आत्मानं ज्ञानाद् अन्येन केनचित्

“Caught in the stream of births and deaths, no one can lift oneself out of it by anything other than knowledge.”

Verse I.15.53

I.15.53

"भिद्यते हृदयग्रन्थिश् छिद्यन्ते सर्वसंशयाः । क्षीयन्ते चास्य कर्माणि तस्मिन् दृष्ट" इति श्रुतेः

“When that is seen, the knot of the heart is severed, all doubts are cut, and one’s actions are exhausted,” says scripture.

Verse I.15.54

I.15.54

ममाहम् इत्य् एतद् अपोह्य सर्वतो विमुक्तदेहं पदम् अम्बरोपमम् । सुदृष्टशास्त्रानुमितिभ्य ईरितं विमुच्यते ऽस्मिन् यदि निश्चितो नरः

“Setting aside ‘I’ and ‘mine’ entirely, one reaches the bodiless state like space. This is taught by scripture and sound reasoning; a person who is certain of it is freed.”

Padyabhāga · Chapter 16: Pārthiva-prakaraṇa

Verse I.16.1

I.16.1

पार्थिवः कठिनो धातुर् द्रवो देहे स्मृतो ऽम्मयः । पक्तिचेष्टावकाशाः स्युर् वह्निवाय्वम्बरोद्भवाः

“The earth element in the body is hard; the water element is fluid. Digestion and movement arise from fire and air; space provides room.”

Verse I.16.2

I.16.2

घ्राणादीनि तदर्थाश् च पृथिव्यादिगुणाः क्रमात् । रूपालोकवद् इष्टं हि सजातीयार्थम् इन्द्रियम्

“Smell and the other senses, together with their objects, correspond in turn to the qualities of earth and the rest; a sense is suited to an object of its own kind, as sight is to form and light.”

Verse I.16.3

I.16.3

बुद्ध्यर्थान्य् आहुर् एतानि वाक्पाण्यादीनि कर्मणे । तद्विकल्पार्थम् अन्तस्थं मन एकादशं भवेत्

“Speech, the hands and the other organs of action serve action; the senses serve cognition. The mind within, the eleventh, organizes their different functions.”

Verse I.16.4

I.16.4

निश्चयार्था भवेद् बुद्धिस् तां सर्वार्थानुभाविनीम् । ज्ञातात्मोक्तः स्वरूपेण ज्योतिषा व्यञ्जयन् सदा

“The intellect determines; it experiences all objects. The knower of the Self is said to reveal it continually by the light of its own nature.”

Verse I.16.5

I.16.5

व्यञ्जकस् तु यथालोको व्यङ्ग्यस्याकारतां गतः । व्यतिकीर्णो ऽप्य् असंकीर्णस् तद्वज् ज्ञः प्रत्ययैः सदा

“As light, though mixed with what it reveals, appears to take on that thing’s form, yet remains unmixed, so the knower is always distinct from the thoughts.”

Verse I.16.6

I.16.6

स्थितो दीपो यथायत्नः प्राप्तं सर्वं प्रकाशयेत् । शब्दाद्याकारबुद्धीर् ज्ञः प्राप्तास् तद्वत् प्रपश्यति

“As a lamp standing still without effort illuminates everything brought before it, the knower sees the cognitions of sound and the rest as they arise.”

Verse I.16.7

I.16.7

शरीरेन्द्रियसंघात आत्मत्वेन गतां धियम् । नित्यात्मज्योतिषा दीप्तां विशिंषन्ति सुखादयः

“Happiness and the rest distinguish the cognition identified with the body and senses; that cognition is lit by the eternal light of the Self.”

Verse I.16.8

I.16.8

शिरोदुःखादिनात्मानं दुःख्य् अस्मीति हि पश्यति । द्रष्टान्यो दुःखिनो दृश्याद् द्रष्टृत्वच् च न दुःख्य् असौ

“Because of a headache and the like, one thinks ‘I am suffering.’ The witness is other than the suffering that is seen, and, as Seer, does not suffer.”

Verse I.16.9

I.16.9

दुःखी स्याद् दुःख्यहंमानाद् दुःखिनो दर्शनान् न वा । संहते ऽङ्गादिभिर् द्रष्टा दुःखी दुःखस्य नैव सः

“Does one suffer because of the thought ‘I am suffering,’ or because one sees someone suffering? The Seer is joined to body and limbs, but is not itself the sufferer.”

Verse I.16.10

I.16.10

चक्षुर्वत् कर्मकर्तृत्वं स्याच् चेन् नानेकम् एव तत् । संहतं च ततो नात्मा द्रष्टृत्वात् कर्मतां व्रजेत्

“If the Self were agent and object of action like the eye, it would be many; because it is a composite, it is not the Self. The witness does not become an object of action.”

Verse I.16.11

I.16.11

ज्ञानयत्नाद्यनेकत्वम् आत्मनो ऽपि मतं यदि । नैकज्ञानगुणत्वात् तु ज्योतिर्वत् तस्य कर्मता

“If many acts of knowing and effort belonged to the Self, it would be many; but the Self has one nature of awareness, like light, and is not an agent.”

Verse I.16.12

I.16.12

ज्योतिषो द्योतकत्वे ऽपि यद्वन् नात्मप्रकाशनम् । भेदे ऽप्य् एवं समत्वाज् ज्ञ आत्मानं नैव पश्यति

“Although light illumines things, it does not illumine itself as an object; so too the knower, though distinct from objects, does not see itself as an object.”

Verse I.16.13

I.16.13

यद्धर्मा यः पदार्थो न तस्यैवेयात् स कर्मताम् । न ह्य् आत्मानं दहत्य् अग्निस् तथा नैव प्रकाशयेत्

“A thing cannot become an object of action for what has the same nature; fire does not burn itself. So the Self does not illumine itself as an object.”

Verse I.16.14

I.16.14

एतेनैवात्मनात्मनो ग्रहो बुद्धेर् निराकृतः । अंशो ऽप्य् एवं समत्वाद् धि निर्भेदत्वान् न युज्यते

“By this reasoning the intellect cannot grasp the Self as an object. A part of the Self cannot do so either, for it is not divided from the whole.”

Verse I.16.15

I.16.15

शून्यतापि न युक्तैवं बुद्धेर् अन्येन दृश्यता । उक्तातो घटवत् तस्याः प्राक् सिद्धेश् च विकल्पतः

“Nor is it reasonable to say the Self is void. The intellect is seen by another, like a pot; before it is established, one can posit many alternatives.”

Verse I.16.16

I.16.16

अविकल्पं तद् अस्त्य् एव यत् पूर्वं स्याद् विकल्पतः । विकल्पोत्पत्तिहेतुत्वाद् यद्य् अस्यैव च कारणम्

“That which is free of conceptual thought exists before thought arises, since it is the cause from which those thoughts arise.”

Verse I.16.17

I.16.17

अज्ञानं कल्पनामूलं संसारस्य नियामकम् । हित्वात्मानं परं ब्रह्म विद्यान् मुक्तं सदाभयम्

“Ignorance is the root of imagination and governs worldly bondage. Setting it aside, know yourself as the supreme Brahman, ever free and fearless.”

Verse I.16.18

I.16.18

जाग्रत्स्वप्नौ तयोर् बीजं सुषुप्ताख्यं तमोमयम् । अन्योन्यस्मिन्न् असत्त्वाच् च नास्तीत्य् एतत् त्रयं त्यजेत्

“Wakefulness and dream, and the darkness called deep sleep that is their seed, are mutually absent; one should relinquish all three.”

Verse I.16.19

I.16.19

आत्मबुद्धिमनश्चक्षुरालोकार्थादिसंकरात् भ्रान्तिः स्याद् आत्मकर्मेति क्रियाणां संनिपाततः

“When the thought of Self is mixed with mind, intellect, eye, light and objects, confusion arises and actions are wrongly attributed to the Self.”

Verse I.16.20

I.16.20

निमीलोन्मीलने स्थाने वायव्ये ते न चक्षुषः । प्रकाशत्वान् मनस्य् एवं बुद्धौ न स्तः प्रकाशतः

“Opening and closing the eyelids are functions of air, not of the eye. Likewise the mind’s and intellect’s activity is not the light of awareness.”

Verse I.16.21

I.16.21

संकल्पाध्यवसायौ तु मनोबुद्ध्योर् यथाक्रमात् । नेतरेतरधर्मत्वं सर्वं चात्मनि कल्पितम्

“Conception and determination belong respectively to the mind and intellect; they are not each other’s properties. Everything is imagined in the Self.”

Verse I.16.22

I.16.22

स्थानावच्छेददृष्टिः स्याद् इन्द्रियाणां तदात्मताम् । गता धीस् तं हि पश्यञ् ज्ञो देहमात्र इवेक्ष्यते

“Because the senses see only from their limited locations, the intellect identifies with them; the knower, seeing this, is regarded as nothing more than the body.”

Verse I.16.23

I.16.23

क्षणिकं हि तद् अत्यर्थं धर्ममात्रं निरन्तरम् । सादृश्याद् दीपवत् तद्धीस् तच्छान्तिः पुरुषार्थता

“That [mental state] is extremely fleeting, only a quality, momentary; because of similarity it seems continuous, like a lamp. Its cessation is held to be the human goal.”

Verse I.16.24

I.16.24

स्वाकारान्यावभासं च येषां रूपादि विद्यते । येषां नास्ति ततश् चान्यत् पूर्वासंगतिर् उच्यते
