“Some things have color and other forms that appear in cognition as their own; things without such forms are said to be different, with no connection to the former.”

Verse I.16.25

I.16.25

बाह्याकारत्वतो ज्ञप्तेः स्मृत्यभावः सदा क्षणात् । क्षणिकत्वाच् च संस्कारं नैवाधत्ते क्वचित् तु धिः

“If cognition always takes an external form, it disappears in an instant and cannot yield memory; being momentary, it leaves no impression anywhere.”

Verse I.16.26

I.16.26

आधारस्याप्य् असत्त्वाच् च तुल्यतानिर्निमित्ततः । स्थाने वा क्षणिकत्वस्य हानं स्यान् न तद् इष्यते

“There is no support for such an impression, and no reason to treat it as equal to the cognition. If it were located in a lasting basis, momentariness would be lost, which is not accepted.”

Verse I.16.27

I.16.27

शान्तेश् चायत्नसिद्धत्वात् साधनोक्तिर् अनर्थिका । एकैकस्मिन् समाप्तत्वाच् छान्तेर् अन्यानपेक्षता

“Since cessation is accomplished without effort, the teaching of a means is pointless; if each moment has already ended, cessation cannot depend on anything else.”

Verse I.16.28

I.16.28

अपेक्षा यदि भिन्ने ऽपि परसंतान इष्यताम् । सर्वार्थे क्षणिके कस्मिंस् तथाप्य् अन्यानपेक्षता

“If one allows relation even with another stream of moments, why would a momentary thing in every respect not depend on another?”

Verse I.16.29

I.16.29

तुल्यकालसमुद्भूताव् इतरेतरयोगिनौ । योगाच् च संस्कृतो यस् तु सो ऽन्यं हीक्षितुम् अर्हति

“Two things arise at the same time and are connected with one another; whatever is conditioned by that connection ought to be able to perceive the other.”

Verse I.16.30

I.16.30

मृषाध्यासस् तु यत्र स्यात् तन्नाशस् तत्र नो मतः । सर्वनाशो भवेद् यस्य मोक्षः कस्य फलं वद

“Where there is a false superimposition, we do not say that it is destroyed in this way. If everything were annihilated, tell me: for whom would liberation be the fruit?”

Verse I.16.31

I.16.31

अस्ति तावत् स्वयं नाम ज्ञानं वात्मान्यद् एव वा । भावाभावज्ञतस् तस्य नाभावस् त्व् अधिगम्यते

“Knowledge exists in itself, or as something other than the Self. Since it knows what exists and what does not, the absence of knowledge cannot itself be known.”

Verse I.16.32

I.16.32

येनाधिगम्यते ऽभावस् तत् सत् स्यात् तन् न चेद् भवेत् । भावाभावानभिज्ञत्वं लोकस्य स्यान् न चेष्यते

“That by which absence is known must exist; if it did not, the world could not know existence and nonexistence. That is not accepted.”

Verse I.16.33

I.16.33

सद् असत् सदसच् चेति विकल्पात् प्राग् यद् इष्यते । तद् अद्वैतं समत्वात् तु नित्यं चान्यद् विकल्पितात्

“Before the alternatives ‘existent,’ ‘nonexistent’ and ‘both’ arise, what is held to be is nondual and equal, eternal; difference is only imagined.”

Verse I.16.34

I.16.34

विकल्पोद्भवतो ऽसत्त्वं स्वप्नदृश्यवद् इष्यताम् । द्वैतस्य प्राग् असत्त्वाच् च सदसत्त्वादिकल्पनात्

“Since concepts arise, their objects are unreal, like what is seen in a dream. Duality was absent before; the ideas of existence and nonexistence are therefore imagined.”

Verse I.16.35

I.16.35

वाचारम्भणशास्त्राच् च विकाराणां ह्य् अभावता । मृत्योः स मृत्युम् इत्यादेर् मम मायेति च स्मृतेः

“Scripture teaches that change is only a matter of words; it says ‘from death to death’ and ‘this is my māyā,’ and the like.”

Verse I.16.36

I.16.36

विशुद्धिश् चात एवास्य विकल्पाच् च विलक्षणात् । उपादेयो न हेयो ऽत आत्मा नान्यैर् अकल्पितः

“The Self is pure because it is distinct from every imagined notion. Therefore it is not something to be taken up or abandoned; it is not imagined by anything else.”

Verse I.16.37

I.16.37

अप्रकाशो यथादित्ये नास्ति ज्योतिःस्वभावतः । नित्यबोधस्वरूपत्वान् नाज्ञानं तद्वद् आत्मनि

“Just as there is no darkness in the sun, whose nature is light, so there is no ignorance in the Self, whose nature is eternal awareness.”

Verse I.16.38

I.16.38

तथाविक्रियरूपत्वान् नावस्थान्तरम् आत्मनः । अवस्थान्तरवत्त्वे हि नाशो ऽस्य स्यान् न संशयः

“The Self does not pass into another state, because it is changeless; if it could change states, it would surely perish.”

Verse I.16.39

I.16.39

मोक्षो ऽवस्थान्तरं यस्य कृतकः स चलो ह्य् अतः । न संयोगो वियोगो वा मोक्षो युक्तः कथंचन

“If liberation were a different state, it would be made and therefore transient. Liberation cannot reasonably be union or separation.”

Verse I.16.40

I.16.40

संयोगस्याप्य् अनित्यत्वाद् वियोगस्य तथैव च । गमनागमने चैव स्वरूपं तु न हीयते

“Union and separation are both impermanent, as are coming and going; one’s own nature, however, is never lost.”

Verse I.16.41

I.16.41

स्वरूपस्यानिमित्तत्वात् सनिमित्ता हि चापरे । अनुपात्तं स्वरूपं हि स्वेनात्यक्तं तथैव च

“One’s own nature has no cause, while other things have causes; it is neither acquired nor relinquished by oneself.”

Verse I.16.42

I.16.42

स्वरूपत्वान् न सर्वस्य त्यक्तुं शक्यो ह्य् अनन्यतः । गृहीतुं वा ततो नित्यो ऽविषयत्वापृथक्त्वतः

“One cannot relinquish what is one’s own nature, since it is not separate; nor can one take it up. It is eternal, not an object, and never apart.”

Verse I.16.43

I.16.43

आत्मार्थत्वाच् च सर्वस्य नित्य आत्मैव केवलः । त्यजेत् तस्मात् क्रियाः सर्वाः साधनैः सह मोक्षवित्

“Everything is for the sake of the Self; the Self alone is eternal. Therefore the knower of liberation should give up all actions and their means.”

Verse I.16.44

I.16.44

आत्मलाभः परो लाभ इति शास्त्रोपपत्तयः । अलाभो ऽन्यात्मलाभस् तु त्यजेत् तस्माद् अनात्मताम्

“Attainment of the Self is the highest attainment taught by scripture and reason. Attainment of another self is no attainment; therefore give up the not-Self.”

Verse I.16.45

I.16.45

गुणानां समभावस्य भ्रंशो न ह्य् उपपद्यते । अविद्यादेः प्रसुप्तत्वान् न चान्यो हेतुर् इष्यते

“A disturbance in the equilibrium of the qualities cannot be accounted for; ignorance is dormant, and no other cause is accepted.”

Verse I.16.46

I.16.46

इतरेतरहेतुत्वे प्रवृत्तिः स्यात् सदा न वा । नियमो न प्रवृत्तीनां गुणेष्व् आत्मनि वा भवेत्

“If the qualities caused one another, they would be either always active or never active; there could be no rule governing activity in the qualities or the Self.”

Verse I.16.47

I.16.47

विशेषो मुक्तबद्धानां तादर्थ्ये च न युज्यते । अर्थार्थिनोश् च संबन्धो नार्थी ज्ञो नेतरो ऽपि वा

“If that were so, there could be no real distinction between the bound and the liberated; nor would the relation between a seeker and the sought make sense. The knower is not a seeker, nor is anyone else.”

Verse I.16.48

I.16.48

प्रधानस्य च पारार्थ्यं पुरुषस्याविकारतः । न युक्तं सांख्यशास्त्रे ऽपि विकारे ऽपि न युज्यते

“Even in the Sāṅkhya teaching, the unconscious Pradhāna’s existence for the sake of the unchanging Puruṣa makes no sense; even if it changed, it would not be reasonable.”

Verse I.16.49

I.16.49

संबन्धानुपपत्तेश् च प्रकृतेः पुरुषस्य च । मिथो ऽयुक्तं तदर्थत्वं प्रधानस्याचितित्वतः

“Since no relation between Prakṛti and Puruṣa can be established, it is not reasonable to say that Pradhāna acts for the sake of Puruṣa, for Pradhāna is unconscious.”

Verse I.16.50

I.16.50

क्रियोत्पत्तौ विनाशित्वं ज्ञानमात्रे च पूर्ववत् । निर्निमित्ते त्व् अनिर्मोक्षः प्रधानस्य प्रसज्यते

“Action arises and perishes; mere cognition is subject to the same difficulty as before. If liberation had no cause, then no liberation would follow for Pradhāna.”

Verse I.16.51

I.16.51

न प्रकाश्यं यथोष्णत्वं ज्ञानेनैवं सुखादयः । एकनीडत्वतो ऽग्राह्याः स्युः कणादादिवर्त्मनाम्

“Heat is not made manifest by cognition; likewise, happiness and the rest cannot be perceived as qualities inhering in one thing, as in the systems of Kaṇāda and others.”

Verse I.16.52

I.16.52

युगपच् चासमेतत्वात् सुखविज्ञानयोर् अपि । मनोयोगैकहेतुत्वाद् अग्राह्यत्वं सुखस्य च

“Happiness and cognition do not arise together, because they are not conjoined; and happiness cannot be grasped when the mind’s connection is its only cause.”

Verse I.16.53

I.16.53

तथान्येषां च भिन्नत्वाद् युगपज्जन्म नेष्यते । गुणानां समवेतत्वं ज्ञानं चेन् न विशेषणात्

“The other qualities are distinct, so simultaneous arising is not accepted. If cognition inheres in them as their qualifier, they are still not distinct in that way.”

Verse I.16.54

I.16.54

ज्ञानेनैव विशेष्यत्वाज् ज्ञानाप्यत्वं स्मृतेस् तथा । सुखं ज्ञातं मयेत्य् एवं तवाज्ञानात्मकत्वतः

“Because it is qualified by cognition, it is known through cognition and remembered as such: ‘I knew happiness.’ Thus your own view makes happiness an object of awareness.”

Verse I.16.55

I.16.55

सुखादेर् नात्मधर्मत्वम् आत्मनस् ते ऽविकारतः । भेदाद् अन्यस्य कस्मान् न मनसो वाविशेषतः

“Happiness and the rest are not properties of the Self, because the Self is changeless. If they are different from it, why should they not be properties of the mind?”

Verse I.16.56

I.16.56

स्यान् मालापरिहार्या तु ज्ञानं चेज् ज्ञेयतां व्रजेत् । युगपद् वापि चोत्पत्तिर् अभ्युपेते ऽन्त इष्यते

“If cognition itself becomes an object of cognition, there would be an endless chain. Or, at the end, you must admit that they arise together.”

Verse I.16.57

I.16.57

अनवस्थान्तरत्वाच् च बन्धो नात्मनि विद्यते । नाशुद्धिश् चाप्य् असङ्गत्वाद् असङ्गो हीति च श्रुतेः

“The Self has no other state, so there is no bondage in it; being unattached, it has no impurity, as scripture says, ‘unattached indeed.’”

Verse I.16.58

I.16.58

सूक्ष्मैकागोचरेभ्यश् च न लिप्यत इति श्रुतेः । एवं तर्हि न मोक्षो ऽस्ति बन्धाभावात् कथंचन

“Scripture says it is unstained even by the subtle [body and its states]. If so, one might object, there can be no liberation, since there is no bondage at all.”

Verse I.16.59

I.16.59

शास्त्रानर्थक्यम् एवं स्यान् न बुद्धेर् भ्रान्तिर् इष्यते । बन्धो मोक्षश् च तन्नाशः स यथोक्तो न चान्यथा

“Then scripture would be pointless. But we accept the intellect’s delusion. Bondage, liberation and the ending of bondage are as explained, and not otherwise.”

Verse I.16.60

I.16.60

बोधात्मज्योतिषा दीप्ता बोधम् आत्मनि मन्यते । बुद्धिर् नान्यो ऽस्ति बोद्धेति सेयं भ्रान्तिर् हि धीगता

“The intellect, lit by the light of awareness, thinks that awareness is within itself; it imagines, ‘There is no knower other than me.’ This delusion belongs to the intellect.”

Verse I.16.61

I.16.61

बोधस्यात्मस्वरूपत्वान् नित्यं तत्रोपचर्यते । अविवेको ऽप्य् अनाद्यो ऽयं संसारो नान्य इष्यते

“Because awareness is the Self’s nature, it is always spoken of as belonging to the Self. This beginningless lack of discrimination alone is called worldly bondage.”

Verse I.16.62

I.16.62

मोक्षस् तन्नाश एव स्यान् नान्यथानुपपत्तितः । येषां वस्त्वन्तरापत्तिर् मोक्षो नाशस् तु तैर् मतः

“Liberation is only the ending of that [ignorance], since no other account is possible. Those who say liberation is attainment of another reality hold that it is destruction.”

Verse I.16.63

I.16.63

अवस्थान्तरम् अप्य् एवम् अविकारान् न युज्यते । विकारे ऽवयवित्वं स्यात् ततो नाशो घटादिवत्

“A different state is likewise impossible for what is changeless. Change would imply parts and therefore destruction, as with a pot.”

Verse I.16.64

I.16.64

तस्माद् भ्रान्तिर् अतो ऽन्या हि बन्धमोक्षादिकल्पनाः । सांख्यकाणादबौद्धानां मीमांसाहतकल्पनाः

“Therefore bondage, liberation and the rest, other than delusion, are only imagined. These are the confused doctrines of Sāṅkhya, Kaṇāda, Buddhists and Mīmāṃsakas.”

Verse I.16.65

I.16.65

शास्त्रयुक्तिविरोधात् ता नादर्तव्याः कदाचन । शक्यन्ते शतशो वक्तुं दोषास् तासां सहस्रशः

“Because they conflict with scripture and reasoning, those views should never be accepted; hundreds and thousands of their faults can be stated.”

Verse I.16.66

I.16.66

अपि निन्दोपपत्तेश् च यान्य् अतो ऽन्यानि चेत्य् अतः । त्यक्त्वातो ह्य् अन्यशास्त्रोक्तीर् मतिं कुर्याद् दृढां बुधः

“Scripture even censures them, and sound reasoning does too. The wise should abandon the claims of other systems and make their understanding firm.”

Verse I.16.67

I.16.67

श्रद्धाभक्ती पुरस्कृत्य हित्वा सर्वम् अनार्जवम् । वेदान्तस्यैव तत्त्वार्थे व्यासस्यापि मतौ तथा

“With faith and devotion foremost, abandoning every form of dishonesty, one should follow the true meaning of Vedānta and the teaching of Vyāsa.”

Verse I.16.68

I.16.68

इति प्रणुन्ना द्वयवादकल्पना निरात्मवादाश् च तथा हि युक्तितः । व्यपेतशङ्काः परवादतः स्थिरा मुमुक्षवो ज्ञानपथे स्युर् इत्य् उत

“Thus the notions of duality and no-Self are refuted by reason. Free of doubts raised by other views, seekers of liberation should stand firm on the path of knowledge.”

Verse I.16.69

I.16.69

स्वसाक्षिकं ज्ञानम् अतीव निर्मलं विकल्पनाभ्यो विपरीतम् अद्वयम् । अवाप्य सम्यग् यदि निश्चितो भवेन् निरन्वयो निर्वृतिम् एति शाश्वतीम्

“Having gained and firmly understood the self-evident knowledge—supremely pure, nondual and contrary to all imagined distinctions—one attains everlasting peace without remainder.”

Verse I.16.70

I.16.70

इदं रहस्यं परमं परायणं व्यपेतदोषैर् अभिमानवर्जितैः । समीक्ष्य कार्या मतिर् आर्जवे सदा न तत्त्वदृक् स्वान्यमतिर् हि कश्चन

“This is the highest secret and final refuge. Those free of faults and pride should examine it; let understanding always be guided by integrity. One who sees the truth has no separate view of his own.”

Verse I.16.71

I.16.71

अनेकजन्मान्तरसंचितैर् नरो विमुच्यते ऽज्ञाननिमित्तपातकैः । इदं विदित्वा परमं हि पावनं न लिप्यते व्योम इवेह कर्मभिः

“After many lives, a person is freed from the sins caused by ignorance. Knowing this supreme purifier, one is no more stained by action than space.”

Verse I.16.72

I.16.72

प्रशान्तचित्ताय जितेन्द्रियाय च प्रहीणदोषाय यथोक्तकारिणे । गुणान्वितायानुगताय सर्वदा प्रदेयम् एतत् सततं मुमुक्षवे

“This should be given to one whose mind is peaceful, senses mastered, faults relinquished, conduct as taught, and character virtuous—one who is devoted and always seeks liberation.”

Verse I.16.73

I.16.73

परस्य देहे न यथाभिमानिता परस्य तद्वत् परमार्थम् ईक्ष्य च । इदं हि विज्ञानम् अतीव निर्मलं संप्राप्य मुक्तो ऽथ भवेच् च सर्वतः

“Just as one does not identify with another person’s body, so too, seeing reality, one should not identify with one’s own. Gaining this exceptionally pure knowledge, one becomes free in every way.”

Verse I.16.74

I.16.74

न हीह लाभो ऽभ्यधिको ऽस्ति कश्चन स्वरूपलाभात् स इत हि नान्यतः । न देयम् ऐन्द्राद् अपि राज्यतो ऽधिकं स्वरूपलाभं त्व् अपरीक्ष्य यत्नतः

“There is no greater gain than attaining one’s own nature, which comes from no other source. Without careful examination, do not give away this knowledge of the Self—even for a kingdom like Indra’s.”

Padyabhāga · Chapter 17: Samyaṅmati-prakaraṇa

Verse I.17.1

I.17.1

आत्मा ज्ञेयः परो ह्य् आत्मा यस्माद् अन्यन् न विद्यते । सर्वज्ञः सर्वदृक् शुद्धस् तस्मै ज्ञेयात्मने नमः

Salutations to the Self that is to be known, the supreme Self beyond which nothing exists, all-knowing, all-seeing and pure.

Verse I.17.2

I.17.2

पदवाक्यप्रमाणज्ञैर् दीपभूतैः प्रकाशितम् । ब्रह्म वेदरहस्यं यैस् तान् नित्यं प्रणतो ऽस्म्य् अहम्

I always bow to those who, like lamps, have illumined the secret of the Veda, Brahman, for those who know words, sentences and the means of knowledge.

Verse I.17.3

I.17.3

यद्वाक्सूर्यांशुसंपातप्रणष्टध्वान्तकल्मषः । प्रणम्य तान् गुरून् वक्ष्ये ब्रह्मविद्याविनिश्चयम्

Having bowed to the teachers whose words, like rays of the sun, destroy the darkness of impurity, I will explain the certain meaning of the knowledge of Brahman.

Verse I.17.4

I.17.4

आत्मलाभात् परो नान्यो लाभः कश्चन विद्यते । यदर्था वेदवादाश् च स्मार्ताश् चापि तु याः क्रियाः

“There is no greater gain than attaining the Self; the Vedic teachings and the rites prescribed by tradition are all for that end.”

Verse I.17.5

I.17.5

आत्मार्थो ऽपि हि यो लाभः सुखायेष्टो विपर्ययः । आत्मलाभः परः प्रोक्तो नित्यत्वाद् ब्रह्मवेदिभिः

“Even a gain desired for one’s own sake and happiness can prove the opposite; knowers of Brahman declare attainment of the Self supreme because it is eternal.”

Verse I.17.6

I.17.6

स्वयं लब्धस्वभावत्वाल् लाभस् तस्य न चान्यतः । अन्यापेक्षस् तु यो लाभः सो ऽन्यदृष्टिसमुद्भवः

“Its attainment is the Self’s own nature, gained by no other means. A gain that depends on something else arises from seeing something else as real.”

Verse I.17.7

I.17.7

अन्यदृष्टिस् त्व् अविद्या स्यात् तन्नाशो मोक्ष उच्यते । ज्ञानेनैव तु सो ऽपि स्याद् विरोधित्वान् न कर्मणा

“Seeing something else is ignorance; its destruction is called liberation. It comes through knowledge alone, since action is not its opposite.”

Verse I.17.8

I.17.8

कर्मकार्यस् त्व् अनित्यः स्याद् अविद्याकामकारणः । प्रमाणं वेद एवात्र ज्ञानस्याधिगमे स्मृतः

“What action produces is impermanent and has ignorance and desire as its cause. The Veda is accepted here as the means of knowledge for attaining knowledge.”

Verse I.17.9

I.17.9

ज्ञानैकार्थपरत्वात् तं वाक्यम् एकं ततो विदुः । एकत्वं ह्य् आत्मनो ज्ञेयं वाक्यार्थप्रतिपत्तितः

“Because a single sentence is devoted to knowledge, they understand it as one teaching; through understanding its meaning, the unity of the Self is to be known.”

Verse I.17.10

I.17.10

वाच्यभेदात् तु तद्भेदः कल्प्यो वाच्यो ऽपि तच्छ्रुतेः । त्रयं त्व् एतत् ततः प्रोक्तं रूपं नाम च कर्म च

“Since words differ, a difference in their referents is imagined, though scripture speaks of them together. Thus the three are taught: form, name and action.”

Verse I.17.11

I.17.11

असद् एतत् त्रयं तस्माद् अन्योन्येन हि कल्पितम् । कृतो वर्णो यथा शब्दाच् छ्रुतो ऽन्यत्र धिया बहिः

“This triad is unreal, each part imagined through the others, like the letters that make a word, heard elsewhere outside the mind.”

Verse I.17.12

I.17.12

दृष्टं चापि यथा रूपं बुद्धेः शब्दाय कल्पते । एवम् एतज् जगत् सर्वं भ्रान्तिबुद्धिविकल्पितम्

“Just as a visible form is imagined by the intellect in the word for it, the entire world is constructed by a deluded intellect.”

Verse I.17.13

I.17.13

असद् एतत् ततो युक्तं सच्चिन्मात्रं न कल्पितम् । वेदश् चापि स एवाद्यो वेद्यश् चान्यस् तु कल्पितः

“This triad is therefore unreal; pure Being and consciousness alone are not imagined. That alone is the primal Veda; what is known as other is imagined.”

Verse I.17.14

I.17.14

येन वेत्ति स वेदः स्यात् स्वप्ने सर्वं तु मायया । येन पश्यति तच् चक्षुः शृणोति श्रोत्रम् उच्यते

“That by which one knows is the Veda; in a dream all is māyā. That by which one sees is called the eye, and that by which one hears, the ear.”

Verse I.17.15

I.17.15

येन स्वप्नगतो वक्ति सा वाग् घ्राणं तथैव च । रसनस्पर्शने चैव मनश् चान्यत् तथेन्द्रियम्

“That by which one speaks in a dream is speech; likewise there are smell, taste, touch, mind and the other faculties.”

Verse I.17.16

I.17.16

कल्प्योपाधिभिर् एवैतद् भिन्नं ज्ञानम् अनेकधा । आधिभेदाद् यथा भेदो मणेर् एकस्य जायते

“This one awareness appears divided in many ways through imagined limiting adjuncts, as one jewel seems many-colored because of the colors around it.”

Verse I.17.17

I.17.17

जाग्रतश् च तथा भेदो ज्ञानस्यास्य विकल्पितः । बुद्धिस्थं व्याकरोत्य् अर्थं भ्रान्त्या तृष्णोद्भवक्रियः

“In waking, too, distinctions in this awareness are imagined; the intellect divides its meaning through delusion, driven by desire and action.”

Verse I.17.18

I.17.18

स्वप्ने यद्वत् प्रबोधे च बहिश् चान्तस् तथैव च । आलेख्याध्ययने यद्वत् तद् अन्योन्यधियोद्भवम्

“As in dream and waking, and likewise in what is within and without, as in looking at a drawing and studying it, one thought arises from another.”

Verse I.17.19

I.17.19

यदायं कल्पयेद् भेदं तत्कामः सन् यथाक्रतुः । यत्कामस् तत्क्रतुर् भूत्वा कृतं यत् तत् प्रपद्यते

“When a person imagines difference, desire arises; shaped by desire, one forms an intention, and then attains what one has acted to obtain.”

Verse I.17.20

I.17.20

अविद्याप्रभवं सर्वम् असत् तस्माद् इदं जगत् । तद्वता दृश्यते यस्मात् सुषुप्ते न च गृह्यते

“This entire world, born of ignorance, is unreal. It is seen by one possessed by ignorance, but is not grasped in deep sleep.”

Verse I.17.21

I.17.21

विद्याविद्ये श्रुतिप्रोक्ते एकत्वान्यधियौ हि नः । तस्मात् सर्वप्रयत्नेन शास्त्रे विद्या विधीयते

“Scripture speaks of knowledge and ignorance: for us they are the understanding of oneness and of difference. Therefore, scripture teaches knowledge as the goal of every effort.”

Verse I.17.22

I.17.22

चित्ते ह्य् आदर्शवद् यस्माच् छुद्धे विद्या प्रकाशते । यमैर् नित्यैश् च यज्ञैश् च तपोभिस् तस्य शोधनम्

“Knowledge shines in a pure mind as in a mirror. Restraints, regular rites and austerities are the means to purify it.”

Verse I.17.23

I.17.23

शारीरादितपः कुर्यात् तद्विशुद्ध्यर्थम् उत्तमम् । मनआदिसमाधानं तत्तद्देहविशोषणम्

“One should undertake bodily austerities and the rest for this supreme purification; steadiness of mind and the rest also make the body and its conditions grow lean.”

Verse I.17.24

I.17.24

दृष्टं जागरितं विद्यात् स्मृतं स्वप्नं तद् एव तु । सुषुप्तं तदभवं च स्वम् आत्मानं परं पदम्

“Know what is seen as waking and what is remembered as dream; deep sleep is their absence, and one’s own Self is the supreme state.”

Verse I.17.25

I.17.25

सुषुप्ताख्यं तमो ऽज्ञानं बीजं स्वप्नप्रबोधयोः । स्वात्मबोधप्रदग्धं स्याद् बीजं दग्धं यथाभवम्

“The darkness called deep sleep, ignorance, is the seed of dream and waking. When knowledge of one’s own Self burns it, the seed is like a scorched seed.”

Verse I.17.26

I.17.26

तद् एवैकं त्रिधा ज्ञेयं मायाबीजं पुनः क्रमात् । मायाव्य् आत्माविकारो ऽपि बहुधैको जलार्कवत्

“That one reality is understood in three ways, in sequence, as the seed of māyā. Though the Self, the possessor of māyā, is unchanged, it appears many, like the sun in water.”

Verse I.17.27

I.17.27

बीजं चैकं यथा भिन्नं प्राणस्वप्नादिभिस् तथा । स्वप्नजाग्रच्छरीरेषु तद्वच् चात्मा जलेन्दुवत्

“As one seed appears divided through life-breath, dream and the rest, so the Self appears in dream and waking bodies like the moon reflected in water.”

Verse I.17.28

I.17.28

मायाहस्तिनम् आरुह्य मायाव्य् एको यथा व्रजेत् । आगच्छंस् तद्वद् एवात्मा प्राणस्वप्नादिगो ऽचलः

“Just as one magician mounts an illusory elephant and seems to travel, so the unmoving Self appears to go about in the states of breath, dream and the rest.”

Verse I.17.29

I.17.29

न हस्ती न तदारूढो मायाव्य् अन्यो यथा स्थितः । न प्राणादि न तद्द्रष्टा तथा ज्ञो ऽन्यः सदा दृशिः

“The magician is neither the elephant nor its rider, but stands apart; likewise the knower, ever the Seer, is neither life-breath nor its witness.”

Verse I.17.30

I.17.30

अबद्धचक्षुषो नास्ति माया मायाविनो ऽपि वा । बद्धाक्षस्यैव सा मायामायाव्य् एव ततो भवेत्

“There is no magic for one whose eyes are unbound, nor even for the magician; the magic appears only to one whose sight is bound, and so does the magician.”

Verse I.17.31

I.17.31

साक्षाद् एव स विज्ञेयः साक्षाद् आत्मेति च श्रुतेः । भिद्यते हृदयग्रन्थिर् न चेद् इत्यादितः श्रुतेः

“The Self should be known directly, for scripture says it is direct: ‘The knot of the heart is cut,’ and the rest.”

Verse I.17.32

I.17.32

अशब्दादित्वतो नास्य ग्रहणं चेन्द्रियैर् भवेत् । सुखादिभ्यस् तथान्यत्वाद् बुद्ध्या वापि कथं भवेत्

“Since it has no sound and the other qualities, the senses cannot grasp it; since it differs from pleasure and the rest, how could the intellect grasp it?”

Verse I.17.33

I.17.33

अदृश्यो ऽपि यथा राहुश् चन्द्रे बिम्बं यथाम्भसि । सर्वगो ऽपि तथैवात्मा बुद्धाव् एव स गृह्यते

“Though unseen, Rāhu is seen in the moon’s disk, and a reflection is seen in water; so too, though all-pervading, the Self is apprehended in the intellect.”

Verse I.17.34

I.17.34

भानोर् बिम्बं यथा चौष्ण्यं जले दृष्टं न चाम्भसः । बुद्धौ बोधो न तद्धर्मस् तथैव स्याद् विधर्मतः

“The sun’s image and heat are seen in water, but are not properties of the water; likewise awareness is seen in the intellect, yet is not its property, because their natures differ.”

Verse I.17.35

I.17.35

चक्षुर्युक्ता धियो वृत्तिर् या तां पश्यन्न् अलुप्तदृक् । दृष्टेर् द्रष्टा भवेद् आत्मा श्रुतेः श्रोता तथा श्रुतेः

“The intellect’s activity joined to the eye is seen by the undiminished Seer. The Self is the Seer of seeing and, as scripture says, the hearer of hearing.”

Verse I.17.36

I.17.36

केवलां मनसो वृत्तिं पश्यन् मन्ता मतेर् अजः । विज्ञातालुप्तशक्तित्वात् तथा शास्त्रं न हीत्य् अतः

“The thinker is unborn, seeing only the intellect’s single activity. The knower’s power is never lost; scripture does not make it an object of thought.”

Verse I.17.37

I.17.37

ध्यायतीत्य् अविकारित्वं तथा लेलायतीत्य् अपि । अत्र स्तेनेति शुद्धत्वं तथानन्वागतं श्रुतेः

“It says ‘as if thinking’ and ‘as if moving’; this does not alter the Self. Scripture also says it is pure, untouched by what does not belong to it.”

Verse I.17.38

I.17.38

शक्त्यलोपात् सुषुप्ते ज्ञस् तथा बोधे ऽविकारतः । ज्ञेयस्यैव विशेषस् तु यत्र वेति श्रुतेर् मतः

“Because the knower’s power is not lost in deep sleep, and it is changeless in waking, the difference scripture speaks of belongs only to what is known.”

Verse I.17.39

I.17.39

व्यवधानाद् धि पारोक्ष्यं लोकदृष्टेर् अनात्मनः । दृष्टेर् आत्मस्वरूपत्वात् प्रत्यक्षं ब्रह्म तत् स्मृतम्

“Ordinary experience calls the not-Self indirect because it is obscured by intervening things; but since seeing is the Self’s own nature, Brahman is known as immediate.”

Verse I.17.40

I.17.40

न हि दीपान्तरापेक्षा यद्वद् दीपप्रकाशने । बोधस्यात्मस्वरूपत्वान् न बोधो ऽन्यस् तथेष्यते

“As a lamp needs no other lamp to reveal its light, awareness, being the Self’s own nature, needs no other awareness.”

Verse I.17.41

I.17.41

विषयत्वं विकारित्वं नानात्वं वा न हीष्यते । न हेयो नाप्य् उपादेय आत्मा नान्येन वा ततः

“The Self is not an object, changing, or many; it is neither to be rejected nor acquired, nor does anything else exist apart from it.”

Verse I.17.42

I.17.42

सबाह्याभ्यन्तरो ऽजीर्णो जन्ममृत्युजरातिगः । अहम् आत्मेति यो वेत्ति कुतो न्व् एव बिभेति सः

“Whoever knows ‘I am the Self, unborn, beyond the body within and without, free of old age and death like space’—what could such a one fear?”

Verse I.17.43

I.17.43

प्राग् एवैतद्विधेः कर्म वर्णित्वादेर् अपोहनात् । तद् अस्थूलादिशास्त्रेभ्यस् तत् त्वम् एवेति निश्चयात्

“Before any injunction to action, one should relinquish caste, stage of life and the rest. From scripture such as ‘not gross,’ one becomes certain: ‘You are that.’”

Verse I.17.44

I.17.44

पूर्वदेहपरित्यागे जात्यादीनां प्रहाणतः । देहस्यैव तु जात्यादिस् तस्याप्य् एवं ह्य् अनात्मता

“When one leaves a former body, birth and status are relinquished; these belong to the body alone, which is likewise not-Self.”

Verse I.17.45

I.17.45

ममाहं चेत्य् अतो ऽविद्या शरीरादिष्व् अनात्मसु । आत्मज्ञानेन हेया स्याद् असुराणाम् इति श्रुतेः

“The ignorance expressed by ‘mine’ and ‘I’ is attached to the body and the rest, which are not-Self; knowledge of the Self removes it, as scripture says of the demonic.”

Verse I.17.46

I.17.46

दशाहाशौचकार्याणां पारिव्राज्ये निवर्तनम् । यथा ज्ञानस्य संप्राप्तौ तद्वज् जात्यादिकर्मणाम्

“Just as rites of impurity after a death cease in the life of renunciation, so too the duties of caste and the rest cease when knowledge is attained.”

Verse I.17.47

I.17.47

यत्कामस् तत्क्रतुर् भूत्वा कृतं त्व् अज्ञः प्रपद्यते । यदा स्वात्मदृशः कामाः प्रमुच्यन्ते ऽमृतस् तदा

“Whatever one desires, that one resolves to become, and what one resolves, one does; when desires lodged in the Self are released, one becomes immortal.”

Verse I.17.48

I.17.48

आत्मरूपविधेः कार्यं क्रियादिभ्यो निवर्तनम् । न साध्यं साधनं वात्मा नित्यतृप्तः श्रुतेर् मतः

“The teaching of the Self’s nature brings actions and the rest to an end. The Self is neither an effect nor a means; scripture says it is ever fulfilled.”

Verse I.17.49

I.17.49

उत्पाद्याप्यविकार्याणि संस्कार्यं च क्रियाफलम् । नातो ऽन्यत् कर्मणः कार्यं त्यजेत् तस्मात् ससाधनम्

“The results of action are things produced, obtained, changed or purified. Action has no other result; therefore one should abandon it together with its means.”

Verse I.17.50

I.17.50

तापान्तत्वाद् अनित्यत्वाद् आत्मार्थत्वाच् च या बहिः । संहृत्यात्मनि तां प्रीतिं सत्यार्थी गुरुम् आश्रयेत्

“Since pleasure from outside ends in pain, is impermanent and exists for the sake of the Self, one who seeks truth should gather that love into the Self and approach a teacher.”

Verse I.17.51

I.17.51

शान्तं प्राज्ञं तथा मुक्तं निष्क्रियं ब्रह्मणि स्थितम् । श्रुतेर् आचार्यवान् वेद तद् विद्धीति स्मृतेस् तथा

“One who is peaceful, wise, free of action and established in Brahman knows through scripture and tradition, ‘You are that’; one should have a teacher.”

Verse I.17.52

I.17.52

स गुरुस् तारयेद् युक्तं शिष्यं शिष्यगुणान्वितम् । ब्रह्मविद्याप्लवेनाशु स्वान्तध्वान्तमहोदधिम्

“Such a teacher quickly carries the qualified disciple, endowed with the qualities of a disciple, across the great ocean of inner darkness on the boat of knowledge of Brahman.”

Verse I.17.53

I.17.53

दृष्टिः स्पृष्टिः श्रुतिर् घ्रातिर् मतिर् विज्ञातिर् एव च । शक्तयो ऽन्याश् च भिद्यन्ते चिद्रूपत्वे ऽप्य् उपाधिभिः

“Seeing, touching, hearing, smelling, thinking, knowing and the other powers are distinguished by their limiting adjuncts, though their nature is consciousness.”

Verse I.17.54

I.17.54

अपायोद्भूतिहीनाभिर् नित्यं दीप्यन् रविर् यथा । सर्वगः सर्वदृक् शुद्धः सर्वं जानाति सर्वदा

“Like the sun, always shining without setting or rising, the Self is all-pervading, all-seeing, pure and always knows everything.”

Verse I.17.55

I.17.55

अन्यदृष्टिः शरीरस्थस् तावन्मात्रो ह्य् अविद्यया । जलेन्द्वाद्युपमाभिस् तु तद्धर्मा च विभाव्यते

“The outward cognition, residing in the body, is limited to that extent through ignorance; by examples such as the moon reflected in water, its properties are attributed to the Self.”

Verse I.17.56

I.17.56

दृष्ट्वा बाह्यं निमील्याथ स्मृत्वा तत् प्रविहाय च । अथोन्मील्यात्मनो दृष्टिं ब्रह्म प्राप्नोत्य् अनध्वगः

“Having seen what is external, then closing the eyes, recalling and relinquishing it, one opens the vision of the Self and reaches Brahman without travelling.”

Verse I.17.57

I.17.57

प्राणाद्य् एवं त्रिकं हित्वा तीर्णो ऽज्ञानमहोदधिम् । स्वात्मस्थो निर्गुणः शुद्धो बुद्धो मुक्तः स्वतो हि सः

“Setting aside the triad beginning with life-breath, one crosses the ocean of ignorance; established in one’s own Self, one is qualityless, pure, awakened and naturally free.”

Verse I.17.58

I.17.58

अजो ऽहं चामरो ऽमृत्युर् अजरो ऽभय एव च । सर्वज्ञः सर्वदृक् शुद्ध इति बुद्धो न जायते

“I am unborn, deathless, beyond death, ageless and fearless; all-knowing, all-seeing and pure. The awakened one is not born.”

Verse I.17.59

I.17.59

पूर्वोक्तं यत् तमोबीजं तन् नास्तीति विनिश्चयः । तदभावे कुतो जन्म ब्रह्मैकत्वं विजानतः

“The seed of darkness spoken of before is absent. For one who knows the oneness of Brahman, how could there be birth?”

Verse I.17.60

I.17.60

क्षीरात् सर्पिर् यथोद्धृत्य क्षिप्तं तस्मिन् न पूर्ववत् । बुद्ध्यादेर् ज्ञस् तथासत्यान् न देही पूर्ववद् भवेत्

“As clarified butter drawn from milk, once removed, does not return to its former state, so the knower does not return to embodiment, because intellect and the rest are unreal.”

Verse I.17.61

I.17.61

सत्यं ज्ञानम् अनन्तं च रसादेः पञ्चकात् परम् । स्याम् अदृश्यादिशास्त्रोक्तम् अहं ब्रह्मेति निर्भयः

“I am Brahman, truth, knowledge and infinity, beyond the five elements beginning with taste, as scripture says ‘unseen’ and the rest; I am fearless.”

Verse I.17.62

I.17.62

यस्माद् भीताः प्रवर्तन्ते वाङ्मनःपावकादयः । तदात्मानन्दतत्त्वज्ञो न बिभेति कुतश्चन

“From that reality all beings—including speech, mind and fire—act out of fear. One who knows the blissful nature of the Self fears nothing.”

Verse I.17.63

I.17.63

नामादिभ्यः परे भूम्नि स्वाराज्ये चेत् स्थितो ऽद्वये । प्रणमेत् कं तदात्मज्ञो न कार्यं कर्मणा तदा

“If one stands in the nondual sovereignty beyond names and the rest, to whom would the knower of the Self bow? No action is required then.”

Verse I.17.64

I.17.64

विराड् वैश्वानरो बाह्यः स्मरन्न् अन्तः प्रजापतिः । प्रविलीने तु सर्वस्मिन् प्राज्ञो ऽव्याकृतम् उच्यते

“The cosmic person is the external waking state; within, the Lord of creatures is remembered. When all has dissolved, the unmanifest is called Prājña.”

Verse I.17.65

I.17.65

वाचारम्भणमात्रत्वात् सुषुप्तादित्रिकं त्व् असत् । सत्यो ज्ञश् चाहम् इत्य् एवं सत्यसन्धो विमुच्यते

“The three states, including deep sleep, are unreal, being only a matter of words. Knowing ‘I am the real Knower,’ one is freed.”

Verse I.17.66

I.17.66

भारूपत्वाद् यथा भानोर् नाहोरात्रे तथैव तु । ज्ञानाज्ञाने न मे स्यातां चिद्रूपत्वाविशेषतः

“As there is no day or night for the radiant sun, so knowledge and ignorance do not exist for me, whose nature is undifferentiated consciousness.”

Verse I.17.67

I.17.67

शास्त्रस्यानतिशङ्क्यत्वाद् ब्रह्मैव स्याम् अहं सदा । ब्रह्मणो मे न हेयं स्याद् ग्राह्यं वेति च संस्मरेत्

“Since scripture cannot be doubted, I am always Brahman alone. I should remember that nothing of Brahman is to be given up or taken up.”

Verse I.17.68

I.17.68

अहम् एव च भूतेषु सर्वेष्व् एको नभो यथा । मयि सर्वाणि भूतानि पश्यन्न् एवं न जायते

“I alone am one in all beings, like space. Seeing all beings in me, one is not born again.”

Verse I.17.69

I.17.69

न बाह्यं मध्यतो वान्तर् विद्यते ऽन्यत् स्वतः क्वचित् । अबाह्यान्तःश्रुतेः किंचित् तस्माच् छुद्धः स्वयंप्रभः

“Nothing other than the Self exists outside, within or in between. Scripture says it is neither outside nor inside; therefore it is pure and self-luminous.”

Verse I.17.70

I.17.70

नेतिनेत्यादिशास्त्रेभ्यः प्रपञ्चोपशमो ऽद्वयः । अविज्ञातादिशास्त्राच् च नैव ज्ञेयो ह्य् अतो ऽन्यथा

“From teachings such as ‘not this, not this,’ the world comes to rest in nonduality; from ‘unknown’ and the rest, it is clear that the Self cannot be known in any other way.”

Verse I.17.71

I.17.71

सर्वस्यात्माहम् एवेति ब्रह्म चेद् विदितं परम् । स आत्मा सर्वभूतानाम् आत्मा ह्य् एषाम् इति श्रुतेः

“If one knows Brahman as ‘I alone am the Self of all,’ the supreme Brahman is known, for scripture says, ‘This is the Self of all beings.’”

Verse I.17.72

I.17.72

जीवश् चेत् परम् आत्मानं स्वात्मानं देवम् अञ्जसा । देवोपास्यः स देवानां पशुत्वाच् च निवर्तते

“If the individual knows the supreme Self, the Lord, directly as its own Self, that person ceases to be a worshipping dependent, like an animal among the gods.”

Verse I.17.73

I.17.73

अहम् एव सदात्मज्ञः शून्यस् त्व् अन्यैर् यथाम्बरम् । इत्य् एवं सत्यसंधत्वाद् असद्धाता न बध्यते

“I alone am ever the knower of the Self; others are empty, like space. One who is true to this is not bound by what is false.”

Verse I.17.74

I.17.74

कृपणास् ते ऽन्यथैवातो विदुर् ब्रह्म परं हि ये । स्वराड् यो ऽनन्यदृक् स्वस्थस् तस्य देवा असन् वशे

“Those who know supreme Brahman otherwise are pitiable. For the self-ruling one, who sees no other and rests in the Self, the gods are as though unreal and under his control.”

Verse I.17.75

I.17.75

हित्वा जात्यादिसंबन्धं वाचो ऽन्याः सह कर्मभिः । ओम् इत्य् एवं स्वम् आत्मानं सर्वं शुद्धं प्रपद्यथ

“Giving up association with caste and the rest, and all other words and actions, attain your own Self, all and pure, through the syllable Om.”

Verse I.17.76

I.17.76

सेतुं सर्वव्यवस्थानाम् अहोरात्रादिवर्जितम् । तिर्यग् ऊर्ध्वम् अधः सर्वं सकृज्ज्योतिर् अनामयम्

“The Self is the bridge that holds together every state, free of day, night and the rest; a single light, without illness, it extends across, above, below and in every direction.”

Verse I.17.77

I.17.77

धर्माधर्मविनिर्मुक्तं भूतभव्यात् कृताकृतात् । स्वम् आत्मानं परं विद्याद् विमुक्तं सर्वबन्धनैः

“Know your own Self as supreme, free from merit and demerit, past and future, what has been done and what has not—free from every bond.”

Verse I.17.78

I.17.78

अकुर्वन् सर्वकृच् छुद्धस् तिष्ठन्न् अत्येति धावतः । मायया सर्वशक्तित्वाद् अजः सन् बहुधा मतः

“Doing nothing, it is the doer of all; pure, it surpasses what runs and what stands still. Through its power over māyā it is unborn, yet is regarded as many.”

Verse I.17.79

I.17.79

राजवत् साक्षिमात्रत्वात् संनिध्याद् भ्रामको यथा । भ्रामयञ् जगद् आत्माहं निष्क्रियो ऽकारको ऽद्वयः

“Like a king who causes activity merely by being present, I, the Self, make the world turn while remaining only its witness—actionless, without agency and nondual.”

Verse I.17.80

I.17.80

निर्गुणं निष्क्रियं नित्यं निर्द्वन्द्वं यन् निरामयम् । शुद्धं बुद्धं तथा मुक्तं तद् ब्रह्मास्मीति धारयेत्

“I am that Brahman which is qualityless, actionless, eternal, free from every pair of opposites, free from illness, pure, awakened and liberated.”

Verse I.17.81

I.17.81

बन्धं मोक्षं च सर्वं यत इदम् उभयं हेयम् एकं द्वयं च । ज्ञेयाज्ञेयाभ्यतीतं परमम् अधिगतं तत्त्वम् एकं विशुद्धम् । विज्ञायैतद् यथावच् छ्रुतिमुनिगदितं शोकमोहाव् अतीतः । सर्वज्ञः सर्वकृत् स्याद् भवभयरहितो ब्राह्मणो ऽवाप्तकृत्यः

“Everything called bondage and liberation is to be set aside. The one supreme, pure reality is beyond both the knowable and unknowable. Knowing it as taught by scripture and sages, one goes beyond grief and delusion, becomes all-knowing and free from fear, and fulfills one’s purpose.”

Verse I.17.82

I.17.82

न स्वयं स्वस्य नान्यश् च नान्यस्यात्मा च हेयगः । उपादेयो न चाप्य् एवम् इति सम्यङ्मतिः स्मृता

“The Self is not rejected by itself, nor is one Self other than another’s; no Self is to be relinquished or taken up. This is rightly understood.”

Verse I.17.83

I.17.83

आत्मप्रत्यायिका ह्य् एषा सर्ववेदान्तगोचरा । ज्ञात्वैतां हि विमुच्यन्ते सर्वसंसारबन्धनैः

“This is the teaching of the Self, the subject of all Vedānta. Knowing it, people are freed from every bond of worldly existence.”

Verse I.17.84

I.17.84

रहस्यं सर्ववेदानां देवानां चापि यत् परम् । पवित्रं परमं ह्य् एतत् तद् एतत् संप्रकाशितम्

“This supreme and sacred truth is the secret of all the Vedas and of the gods; now it has been fully revealed.”

Verse I.17.85

I.17.85

नैतद् देयम् अशान्ताय रहस्यं ज्ञानम् उत्तमम् । विरक्ताय प्रदातव्यं शिष्यायानुगताय च

“This highest secret knowledge must not be given to one who is not peaceful; it should be given to a devoted disciple who is dispassionate.”

Verse I.17.86

I.17.86

ददतश् चात्मनो ज्ञानं निष्क्रियो ऽन्यो न विद्यते । ज्ञानम् इच्छन् भवेत् तस्माद् युक्तः शिष्यगुणैः सदा

“There is no other, inactive Self apart from the one who gives knowledge of the Self; therefore one who desires knowledge should always be equipped with the qualities of a disciple.”

Verse I.17.87

I.17.87

ज्ञानं ज्ञेयं तथा ज्ञाता यस्माद् अन्यन् न विद्यते । सर्वज्ञः सर्वशक्तिर् यस् तस्मै ज्ञानात्मने नमः

“Since knowledge, what is known and the knower are not other than this, salutations to that Self whose nature is knowledge, all-knowing and all-powerful.”

Verse I.17.88

I.17.88

विद्यया तारिताः स्मो यैर् जन्ममृत्युमहोदधिम् । सर्वज्ञेभ्यो नमस् तेभ्यो गुरुभ्यो ऽज्ञानसंकुलम्

“Salutations to those all-knowing teachers who ferried us across the great ocean of birth and death, crowded with ignorance, through knowledge.”

Padyabhāga · Chapter 18: Tat-tvam-asi-prakaraṇa

Verse I.18.1

I.18.1

येनात्मना विलीयन्त उद्भवन्ति च वृत्तयः । नित्यावगतये तस्मै नमो धीप्रत्ययात्मने

Salutations to that eternal awareness, the nature of every cognition, in which thoughts arise and dissolve.

Verse I.18.2

I.18.2

प्रमथ्य वज्रोपमयुक्तिसंभृतैः श्रुतेर् अरातिञ् शतशो वचो ऽसिभिः । ररक्ष वेदार्थनिधिं विशालधीर् नमो यतीन्द्राय गुरोर् गरीयसे

“With words like swords, drawn from reasoning as strong as a thunderbolt, he struck down the enemies of scripture. I bow to that great-minded king of renunciants, the teacher above all, who protected the vast treasure of the Veda’s meaning.”

Verse I.18.3

I.18.3

नित्यमुक्तः सद् एवास्मीत्य् एवं चेन् न भवेन् मतिः । किमर्थं श्रावयत्य् एवं मातृवच् छ्रुतिर् आदृता

“If one did not have the conviction ‘I am Being, eternally free,’ why would scripture lovingly teach it like a mother?”

Verse I.18.4

I.18.4

सिद्धाद् एवाहम् इत्य् अस्माद् युष्मद्धर्मो निषिध्यते । रज्ज्वाम् इवाहिधीर् युक्त्या तत् त्वम् इत्यादिशासनैः

“From the established knowledge ‘I am,’ the properties of ‘you’ are negated through teachings such as ‘you are that,’ just as the mistaken snake is negated from the rope by reasoning.”

Verse I.18.5

I.18.5

शास्त्रप्रामाण्यतो ज्ञेया धर्मादेर् अस्तिता यथा । विषापोहो यथा ध्यानाद् ध्नुतिः स्यात् पाप्मनस् तथा

“As the existence of duty and the rest is known from scripture, so too one’s sin is removed through meditation, as poison is neutralized.”

Verse I.18.6

I.18.6

सद् ब्रह्माहं करोमीति प्रत्ययाव् आत्मसाक्षिकौ । तयोर् अज्ञानजस्यैव त्यागो युक्ततरो मतः

“The thoughts ‘I am Being, Brahman’ and ‘I act’ are both witnessed by the Self; it is more reasonable to relinquish only the latter, which arises from ignorance.”

Verse I.18.7

I.18.7

सद् अस्मीति प्रमाणोत्था धीर् अन्या तन्निभोद्भवा । प्रत्यक्षादिनिभा वापि बाध्यते दिग्भ्रमादिवत्

“The cognition ‘I am Being’ arises from a means of knowledge; another cognition, though similar, or one born of perception, is sublated like mistaking direction.”

Verse I.18.8

I.18.8

कर्ता भोक्तेति यच् छास्त्रं लोकबुद्ध्यनुवादि तत् । सद् अस्मीति श्रुतेर् जाता बाध्यते ऽन्यैतयैव धीः

“Scriptural statements about the doer and enjoyer merely restate the world’s common view. The cognition ‘I am Being,’ born from scripture, sublates those and the other views.”

Verse I.18.9

I.18.9

सद् एव त्वम् असीत्य् उक्ते नात्मनो मुक्ततां स्थिराम् । प्रपद्यते प्रसंचक्षाम् अतो युक्त्यानुचिन्तयेत्

“Even when told ‘You are Being,’ one may not gain firm knowledge of the Self’s freedom merely by hearing; one should therefore reflect with reasoning.”

Verse I.18.10

I.18.10

सकृद् उक्तं न गृह्णाति वाक्यार्थज्ञो ऽपि यो भवेत् । अपेक्षते ऽत एवान्यद् अवोचाम द्वयं हि तत्

“Even a person who understands the sentence meaning may not grasp it when it is stated only once; for that reason I have explained the two sides and the need for further teaching.”

Verse I.18.11

I.18.11

नियोगो ऽप्रतिपन्नत्वात् कर्मणां स यथा भवेत् । अविरुद्धो भवेत् तावद् यावत् संवेद्यतादृढा

“An injunction may be valid for actions so long as the truth has not been firmly understood; until then, one’s knowledge remains incomplete.”

Verse I.18.12

I.18.12

चेष्टितं च तथा मिथ्या स्वच्छन्दः प्रतिपद्यते । प्रसंख्यानम् अतः कार्यं यावद् आत्मानुभूयते

“Conduct and the rest are likewise mistaken; one may freely take up repeated contemplation until the Self is directly experienced.”

Verse I.18.13

I.18.13

सद् अस्मीति च विज्ञानम् अक्षजो बाधते ध्रुवम् । शब्दोत्थं दृढसंस्कारो दोषैश् चाक्र्ष्यते बहिः

“The senses will surely oppose the cognition ‘I am Being’; strong impressions arising from sound cognition pull the mind outward.”

Verse I.18.14

I.18.14

श्रुतानुमानजन्मानौ सामान्यविषयौ यतः । प्रत्ययाव् अक्षजो ऽवश्यं विशेषार्थो निवारयेत्

“Knowledge born of scripture and inference has a general subject; therefore perception, which concerns a particular, can sublate it.”

Verse I.18.15

I.18.15

वाक्यार्थप्रत्ययि कश्चिन् निर्दुःखो नोपलभ्यते । यदि वा दृश्यते कश्चिद् वाक्यार्थश्रुतिमात्रतः

“No person who understands the sentence meaning is seen to be free from sorrow. Or, if someone is seen to be free immediately from merely hearing the sentence—”

Verse I.18.16

I.18.16

निर्दुःखो ऽतीतदेहेषु कृतभावो ऽनुमीयते । चर्या नो ऽशास्त्रसंवेद्या स्याद् अनिष्टं तथा सति

“—one should infer that person’s cultivation in former lives; otherwise that person’s conduct would be beyond scripture, an undesirable result.”

Verse I.18.17

I.18.17

सद् असीति फलं चोक्त्वा विधेयं साधनं यतः । न तद् अन्यत् प्रसंख्यानात् प्रसिद्धार्थम् इहेष्यते

“Since the sentence states its result, ‘You are Being,’ what further means is to be enjoined? Nothing beyond repeated contemplation of the meaning already made known is proposed here.”

Verse I.18.18

I.18.18

तस्माद् अनुभवायैव प्रसंचक्षीत यत्नतः । त्यजन् साधनतत्साध्यविरुद्धं शमनादिमान्

“Therefore, for direct experience one should strive and reflect, practising calm and the rest, and giving up whatever means or results conflict with it.”

Verse I.18.19

I.18.19

नैतद् एवं रहस्यानां नेतिनेत्यवसानतः । क्रियासाध्यं पुरा श्राव्यं न मोक्षो नित्यसिद्धतः

“This is not so: the secret teachings end with ‘not this, not this.’ Action is taught beforehand, but liberation is eternally accomplished.”

Verse I.18.20

I.18.20

पुत्रदुःखं यथाध्यस्तं पित्रादुःखे स्व आत्मनि । अहंकर्त्रा तथाध्यस्तं नित्यादुःखे स्व आत्मनि

“As a father’s grief is imagined from grief for his son, so the ego’s agency is superimposed on the Self, which is eternally free of sorrow.”

Verse I.18.21

I.18.21

सो ऽध्यासो नेति नेतीति प्राप्तवत् प्रतिषिध्यते । भूयो ऽध्यासविधिः कश्चित् कुतश्चिन् नोपपद्यते

“That superimposition is negated by the teaching ‘not this, not this’; no further injunction to superimpose anything can be justified.”

Verse I.18.22

I.18.22

आत्मनीह यथाध्यासः प्रतिषेधस् तथैव च । मलाध्यासनिषेधौ खे क्रियेते च यथाबुधैः

“Just as the superimposition on the Self is negated, so too is its denial carried out here, as the ignorant imagine and remove a stain from space.”

Verse I.18.23

I.18.23

प्राप्तश् चेत् प्रतिषिध्येत मोक्षो ऽनित्यो भवेद् ध्रुवम् । अतो ऽप्राप्तनिषेधो ऽयं दिव्य् अग्निचयनादिवत्

“If something already attained were negated, liberation would surely be impermanent. This is therefore a denial of what was never attained, like the prescribed arrangement of a sacred fire.”

Verse I.18.24

I.18.24

संभाव्यो गोचरे शब्दः प्रत्ययो वा न चान्यथा । न संभाव्यौ तदात्मत्वाद् अहंकर्तुस् तथैव च

“A word or cognition can concern only what lies within its scope, and not otherwise; since the Self is the very nature of the agent, it cannot be made an object.”

Verse I.18.25

I.18.25

अहंकर्त्रात्मनि न्यस्तं चैतन्ये कर्तृतादि यत् । नेति नेतीति तत् सर्वं साहंकर्त्रा निषिध्यते

“Agency and the rest, which have been placed on the Self and consciousness, are all negated together with the agent through ‘not this, not this.’”

Verse I.18.26

I.18.26

उपलब्धिः स्वयंज्योतिर् दृशिः प्रत्यक्सदक्रियः । साक्षात् सर्वान्तरः साक्षी चेता नित्यो ऽगुणो ऽद्वयः

“Realization is self-luminous awareness: the inward Seer, Being itself, actionless, the witness, conscious, eternal, without qualities and nondual.”

Verse I.18.27

I.18.27

संनिधौ सर्वदा तस्य स्यात् तदाभो ऽभिमानकृत् । आत्मात्मीयं द्वयं चातः स्याद् अहंममगोचरः

“In its constant presence, the ego creates an appearance like itself; thus the duality ‘I’ and ‘mine’ falls within the ego’s domain.”

Verse I.18.28

I.18.28

जातिकर्मादिमत्त्वाद् धि तस्मिञ् शब्दास् त्व् अहंकृति । न कश्चिद् वर्तते शब्दस् तदभावात् स्व आत्मनि

“Because they apply to caste, action and the rest, words refer to the ego; no word refers to the Self, since none of those conditions belongs to it.”

Verse I.18.29

I.18.29

आभासो यत्र तत्रैव शब्दाः प्रत्यग्दृशिं स्थिताः । लक्षयेयुर् न साक्षात् तम् अभिदध्युः कथंचन

“Where a reflection appears, words established for that reflection can only point toward the inward Seer; they could never denote the Seer directly.”

Verse I.18.30

I.18.30

नह्य् अजात्यादिमान् कश्चिद् अर्थः शब्दैर् निरूप्यते । आत्माभासो यतो ऽहंकृद् आत्मशब्दैस् तथोच्यते

“No object lacking birth, caste and the rest is denoted by words. Since the ego is a reflection of the Self, words speak of it as the Self.”

Verse I.18.31

I.18.31

उल्मुकादौ यथाग्न्यर्थाः परार्थत्वान् न चाञ्जसा । मुखाद् अन्यो मुखाभासो यथादर्शानुकारतः

“Words for fire may be used of a firebrand for another purpose; the face, however, is distinct from its reflection, which appears by imitation in a mirror.”

Verse I.18.32

I.18.32

आभासान् मुखम् अप्य् एवम् आदर्शाननुवर्तनात् । अहंकृत्य् आत्मनिर्भासो मुखाभासवद् इष्यते

“The face is present, and the image appears in the mirror by following it; so the reflection of the Self in the ego is understood like the reflection of a face.”

Verse I.18.33

I.18.33

मुखवत् स्थित आत्मान्यो ऽविविक्तौ तौ तथैव च । संसारी च स इत्य् एक आभासो यस् त्व् अहंकृति

“The Self stands as the face, and the ego as its image; because the two are not discriminated, that single appearance is called a person in bondage.”

Verse I.18.34

I.18.34

वस्तु च्छाया स्मृतेर् अन्यन् माधुर्यादि च कारणम् । ज्ञैकदेशो विकारो वा तदाभासाश्रयः परे

“The object, its shadow, memory and a cause such as sweetness—or a portion or modification of the knower—are offered as the basis of that reflection by other thinkers.”

Verse I.18.35

I.18.35

अहंकर्तैव संसारी स्वतन्त्र इति केचन । अहंकारादिसंतानः संसारी नान्वयि पृथक्

“Some say the ego alone is the independent person in bondage; for them the stream of ego-moments has no enduring continuity.”

Verse I.18.36

I.18.36

इत्य् एवं सौगता आहुस् तत्र न्यायो विचार्यताम् । संसारिणां कथा त्व् आस्तां प्रकृतं त्व् अधुनोच्यते

“Thus the Buddhists speak. Let their reasoning be examined; we will leave aside their account of the person in bondage and return to the present subject.”

Verse I.18.37

I.18.37

मुखाभासो य आदर्शे धर्मो नान्यतरस्य सः । द्वयोर् एकस्य चेद् धर्मो वियुक्ते ऽन्यतरे भवेत्

“The reflection of a face in a mirror is not a property of either one alone; if it belonged to one, it would remain even when separated from the other.”

Verse I.18.38

I.18.38

मुखेन व्यपदेशात् स मुखस्यैवेति चेन् मतम् । नादर्शानुविधानाच् च मुखे सत्य् अविभावतः

“If it is called the face’s reflection because it is described in relation to the face, that is not so: it follows the mirror, and vanishes when the face is absent.”

Verse I.18.39

I.18.39

द्वयोर् एवेति चेत् तन् न द्वयोर् एवाप्य् अदर्शनात् । अदृष्टस्य सतो दृष्टिः स्याद् राहोश् चन्द्रसूर्ययोः

“If you say it belongs to both, that too is wrong, since neither of them is itself the reflection; the unseen can be seen, as in the case of Rāhu and the sun or moon.”

Verse I.18.40

I.18.40

राहोः प्राग् एव वस्तुत्वं सिद्धं शास्त्रप्रमाणतः । छायापक्षे त्व् अवस्तुत्वं तस्य स्यात् पूर्वयुक्तितः

“Rāhu’s existence is established by scripture even before it is seen; if it were only a shadow, then by the reasoning already given it would be unreal.”

Verse I.18.41

I.18.41

छायाक्रान्तेर् निषेधो ऽयं न तु वस्तुत्वसाधकः । न ह्य् अर्थान्तरनिष्ठं सद् वाक्यम् अर्थान्तरं वदेत्

“This denial of an eclipse or covering does not establish Rāhu as a real object; a sentence whose subject is one thing cannot directly speak of another.”

Verse I.18.42

I.18.42

माधुर्यादि च यत् कार्यम् उष्णद्रव्याद्यसेवनात् । छायाया न त्व् अदृष्टत्वाद् अपाम् एव च दर्शनात्

“Effects such as sweetness come from consuming foods and substances; they do not arise from an unseen shadow, while water alone is actually seen.”

Verse I.18.43

I.18.43

आत्माभासाश्रयाश् चैवं मुखाभासाश्रया यथा । गम्यते शास्त्रयुक्तिभ्याम् आभासासत्त्वम् एव च

“Likewise, scripture and reasoning show that the reflection is unreal; the Self is its basis, as the face is the basis of its image in a mirror.”

Verse I.18.44

I.18.44

न दृशेर् अविकारित्वाद् आभासस्याप्य् अवस्तुतः । नाचितित्वाद् अहंकर्तुः कस्य संसारिता भवेत्

“Since the Seer is changeless and the reflection unreal, and since the ego is insentient, who could be a person in bondage?”

Verse I.18.45

I.18.45

अविद्यामात्र एवातः संसारो ऽस्त्व् अविवेकतः । कूटस्थेनात्मना नित्यम् आत्मवान् आत्मनीव सः

“Therefore bondage is only ignorance, a failure to discriminate; it seems to belong to the Self, which is changeless, as though it were real in the Self.”

Verse I.18.46

I.18.46

रज्जुसर्पो यथा रज्ज्वा सात्मकः प्राग् विवेकतः । अवस्तुसन्न् अपि ह्य् एष कूटस्थेनात्मना तथा

“A snake depends on the rope before discrimination; though unreal, it appears to rest on that ground. So too this world rests on the changeless Self.”

Verse I.18.47

I.18.47

आत्माभासाश्रयश् चात्मा प्रत्ययैः स्वैर् विकारवान् । सुखी दुःखी च संसारी नित्य एवेति केचन

“Some say that the Self, as the ground of its reflection, changes with its own thoughts and becomes happy or unhappy, a person in bondage, though it is eternal.”

Verse I.18.48

I.18.48

आत्माभासापरिज्ञानाद् याथात्म्येन विमोहिताः । अहंकर्तारम् आत्मेति मन्यन्ते ते निरागमाः

“Confused and rejecting scripture, they mistake the ego, the doer, for the Self because they do not understand the reflection of the Self.”

Verse I.18.49

I.18.49

संसारो वस्तुसंस् तेषां कर्तृभोक्तृत्वलक्षणः । आत्माभासाश्रयाज्ञानात् संसरन्त्य् अविवेकतः

“For them bondage is real, marked by agency and enjoyment; through ignorance of the basis of the Self’s reflection, they wander without discrimination.”

Verse I.18.50

I.18.50

चैतन्याभासता बुद्धेर् आत्मनस् तत्स्वरूपता । स्याच् चेत् तं ज्ञानशब्दैश् च वेदः शास्तीति युज्यते

“If the intellect is a reflection of consciousness and the Self is consciousness itself, it makes sense for the Veda to teach it through words such as ‘knowledge.’”

Verse I.18.51

I.18.51

प्रकृतिप्रत्ययार्थौ यौ भिन्नाव् एकाश्रयौ यथा । करोति गच्छतीत्यादौ दृष्टौ लोकप्रसिद्धितः

“In ordinary usage, the different meanings of root and suffix can share one basis, as in expressions such as ‘he makes’ and ‘he goes.’”

Verse I.18.52

I.18.52

नानयोर् द्व्याश्रयत्वं तु लोके दृष्टं स्मृतौ तथा । जानात्यर्थेषु को हेतुर् द्व्याश्रयत्वे निगद्यताम्

“Ordinary speech and memory do not show two separate bases for them. Explain the reason for assigning two bases to words such as ‘knows.’”

Verse I.18.53

I.18.53

आत्माभासस् तु तिङ्वाच्यो धात्वर्थश् च धियः क्रिया । उभयं चाविवेकेन जानातीत्य् उच्यते मृषा

“The reflected Self is what the verbal ending signifies; the action of the intellect is the root’s meaning. Through lack of discrimination, both are falsely called ‘knowing.’”

Verse I.18.54

I.18.54

न बुद्धेर् अवबोधो ऽस्ति नात्मनो विद्यते क्रिया । अतो नान्यतरस्यापि जानातीति च युज्यते

“The intellect has no awareness, and the Self performs no action; therefore it makes no sense to say that either one separately ‘knows.’”

Verse I.18.55

I.18.55

नाप्य् अतो भावशब्देन ज्ञप्तिर् इत्य् अपि युज्यते । न ह्य् आत्मा विक्रियामात्रो नित्य आत्मेतिशासनात्

“Nor does it make sense to treat knowledge as a state or modification. The Self is not merely a change, for scripture teaches that it is the eternal Self.”

Verse I.18.56

I.18.56

न बुद्धेर् बुद्धिवाच्यत्वं करणं न ह्य् अकर्तृकम् । नापि ज्ञायत इत्य् एवं कर्मशब्दैर् निरुच्यते

“The intellect is not denoted as a knower, for it is an instrument and cannot act without an agent; nor is it described by the word ‘known.’”

Verse I.18.57

I.18.57

न येषाम् एक एवात्मा निर्दुःखो ऽविक्रियः सदा । तेषां स्याच् छब्दवाच्यत्वं ज्ञेयत्वं चात्मनः सदा

“Those who accept only one Self, ever free from sorrow and change, do not hold that the Self is always denoted by words or made an object of knowledge.”

Verse I.18.58

I.18.58

यदाहंकर्तुर् आत्मत्वं तदा शब्दार्थमुख्यता । नाशनायादिमत्त्वात् तु श्रुतौ तस्यात्मतेष्यते

“If the ego were the Self, the word’s primary meaning would apply; but since it has a beginning and can perish, scripture does not accept its Selfhood.”

Verse I.18.59

I.18.59

हन्त तर्हि न मुख्यार्थो नापि गौणः कथंचन । जानातीत्यादिशब्दस्य गतिर् वाच्या तथापि तु

“Then what is the force of the word ‘knows’—neither literal nor figurative? Its proper usage must still be explained.”

Verse I.18.60

I.18.60

शब्दानाम् अयथार्थत्वे वेदस्याप्य् अप्रमाणता । सा च नेष्टा ततो ग्राह्या गतिर् अस्य प्रसिद्धितः

“If words did not convey their meanings accurately, the Veda too would be an invalid means of knowledge. That is not acceptable; therefore its usage must be understood as established.”

Verse I.18.61

I.18.61

प्रसिद्धिर् मूढलोकस्य यदि ग्राह्या निरात्मता । लोकायतिकसिद्धान्तः स चानिष्टः प्रसज्यते

“If the usage of ordinary, deluded people alone were accepted, the doctrine that there is no Self would follow—the unwanted conclusion of the materialists.”

Verse I.18.62

I.18.62

अभियुक्तप्रसिद्धिश् चेत् पूर्ववद् दुर्विवेकता । गतिशून्यं न वेदो ऽयं प्रमाणं संवदत्य् उत

“If the usage of scholars is accepted, the old failure to discriminate remains; the Veda would then have no coherent meaning and would not convey valid knowledge.”

Verse I.18.63

I.18.63

आदर्शमुखसामान्यं मुखस्येष्टं हि मानवैः । मुखस्य प्रतिबिम्बो हि मुखाकारेण दृश्यते

“People accept the resemblance between a face and its image in a mirror; the reflection is seen to have the face’s form.”

Verse I.18.64

I.18.64

यत्र यस्यावभासस् तु तयोर् एवाविवेकतः । जानातीति क्रियां सर्वो लोको वक्ति स्वभवतः

“Where one thing appears in another, everyone naturally says ‘it knows,’ because the two are not distinguished.”

Verse I.18.65

I.18.65

बुद्धेः कर्तृत्वम् अध्यस्य जानातीति ज्ञ उच्यते । तथा चैतन्यम् अध्यस्य ज्ञत्वं बुद्धेर् इहोच्यते

“Agency is superimposed on the intellect, and so the knower is called one who ‘knows’; consciousness is likewise superimposed on the intellect, which is then called aware.”

Verse I.18.66

I.18.66

स्वरूपं चात्मनो ज्ञानं नित्यं ज्योतिः श्रुतेर् यतः । न बुद्ध्या क्रियते तस्मान् नात्मनान्येन वा सदा

“Awareness is the Self’s own nature, an eternal light, as scripture teaches; it is not produced by the intellect, nor by the Self through any other means.”

Verse I.18.67

I.18.67

देहे ऽहंप्रत्ययो यद्वज् जानातीति च लौकिकाः । वदन्ति ज्ञानकर्तृत्वं तद्वद् बुद्धेस् तथात्मनः

“Just as ordinary people say ‘I’ of the body, they speak of the intellect and Self as agents of knowing, through the same mistaken attribution.”

Verse I.18.68

I.18.68

बौद्धैस् तु प्रत्ययैर् एवं क्रियमाणैश् च चिन्निभैः । मोहिताः क्रियते ज्ञानम् इत्य् आहुस् तार्किका जनाः

“Because perceptions are produced in this way and resemble consciousness, Buddhist logicians say that cognition itself is produced.”

Verse I.18.69

I.18.69

तस्माज् ज्ञाभासबुद्धीनाम् अविवेकात् प्रवर्तिताः । जानातीत्यादिशब्दश् च प्रत्ययो या च तत्स्मृतिः

“Words such as ‘knows,’ the cognition and its memory arise through failure to distinguish the reflected knower from the intellect.”

Verse I.18.70

I.18.70

आदर्शानुविधायित्वं छायाया अस्यते मुखे । बुद्धिधर्मानुकारित्वं ज्ञाभासस्य तथेष्यते

“The reflection follows the mirror, not the face; in the same way, the reflection of the knower is held to follow the properties of the intellect.”

Verse I.18.71

I.18.71

बुद्धेस् तु प्रत्ययास् तस्माद् आत्माभासेन दीपिताः । ग्राहका इव भासन्ते दहन्तीवोल्मुकादयः

“The intellect’s perceptions, lit by the reflected Self, appear to be knowers, as firebrands seem to burn.”

Verse I.18.72

I.18.72

स्वयम् एवावभास्यन्ते ग्राहकाः स्वयम् एव च । इत्य् एवं ग्राहकास्तित्वं प्रतिषेधन्ति सौगताः

“The Buddhists deny a separate perceiver: perceptions themselves appear and grasp their objects.”

Verse I.18.73

I.18.73

यद्य् एवं नान्यदृश्यास् ते किं तद्वारणम् उच्यताम् । भावाभावौ हि तेषां यौ नान्यग्राह्यौ सदा यदि

“If this is so, they are not seen by anything else; tell us what refutes that. Their existence and nonexistence are never objects of another knower.”

Verse I.18.74

I.18.74

अन्वयी ग्राहकस् तेषाम् इत्य् एतद् अपि तत्समम् । अचितित्वस्य तुल्यत्वाद् अन्यस्मिन् ग्राहके सति

“The claim that an enduring perceiver is present in them has the same fault, if that perceiver is as insentient as another object.”

Verse I.18.75

I.18.75

अध्यक्षस्य समीपे तु सिद्धिः स्याद् इति चेन् मतम् । नाध्यक्षे ऽनुपकारित्वाद् अन्यत्रापि प्रसङ्गतः

“If you say they are established by the direct witness, that does not work: the witness gains nothing from them, and the same problem follows for anything else.”

Verse I.18.76

I.18.76

अर्थी दुःखी च यः श्रोता स त्व् अध्यक्षो ऽथवेतरः । अध्यक्षस्य च दुःखित्वम् अर्थित्वं च न ते मतम्

“The listener who seeks happiness and is suffering—are these the witness or something else? You do not hold that the witness itself desires or suffers.”

Verse I.18.77

I.18.77

कर्ताध्यक्षं सद् अस्मीति नैव सद्ग्रहम् अर्हति । सद् एवासीति मिथ्योक्तिः श्रुतेर् अपि न युज्यते

“The thought ‘I am the doer’ cannot qualify the Self as Being. Then the teaching ‘You are Being’ would be false, even according to scripture.”

Verse I.18.78

I.18.78

अविविच्योभयं वक्ति श्रुतिश् चेत् स्याद् ग्रहस् तथा । अस्मदस् तु विविच्यैव त्वम् एवेति वदेद् यदि

“If scripture spoke of the two without distinguishing them, it would be a mere seizure of both; but if it spoke distinctly, saying ‘you alone are that’…”

Verse I.18.79

I.18.79

प्रत्ययान्वयिनिष्ठत्वम् उक्तो दोषः प्रसज्यते । त्वम् इत्य् अध्यक्षनिष्ठश् चेद् अहमध्यक्षयोः कथम्

“…the fault already stated would follow: it would be grounded in a connected cognition. If ‘you’ refers to the witness, how could there be a relation between ego and witness?”

Verse I.18.80

I.18.80

संबन्धो वाच्य एवात्र येन त्वम् इति लक्षयेत् । द्रष्टृदृश्यत्वसंबन्धो यद्य् अध्यक्षे ऽक्रिये कथम्

“A relation must be stated by which ‘you’ can be indicated. If it is the relation of seer and seen, how could that belong to the inactive witness?”

Verse I.18.81

I.18.81

अक्रियत्वे ऽपि तादात्म्यम् अध्यक्षस्य भवेद् यदि । आत्माध्यक्षो ममास्तीति संबन्धाग्रहणे न धीः

“If there is identity even though the witness is inactive, then without grasping a relation there could be no cognition ‘I have the Self as witness.’”

Verse I.18.82

I.18.82

संबन्धग्रहणं शास्त्राद् इति चेन् मन्यसे न हि । पूर्वोक्ताः स्युस् त्रिधा दोषा ग्रहो वा स्यान् ममेति च

“If you say the relation is grasped from scripture, the three faults already explained still follow, or else there would be the thought ‘it is mine.’”

Verse I.18.83

I.18.83

अदृशिर् दृशिरूपेण भाति बुद्धिर् यदा सदा । प्रत्यया अपि तस्याः स्युस् तप्तायोविस्फुलिङ्गवत्

“When the intellect appears in the form of the Seer, its thoughts are like sparks from heated iron.”

Verse I.18.84

I.18.84

आभासस् तदभावश् च दृशेः सीम्नो न चान्यथा । लोकस्य युक्तितः स्यातां तद्ग्रहश् च तथा सति

“Reflection and its absence alike belong only within the boundary of the Seer; through ordinary usage and reasoning one understands its grasp.”

Verse I.18.85

I.18.85

नन्व् एवं दृशिसंक्रान्तिर् अयःपिण्डे ऽग्निवद् भवेत् । मुखाभासवद् इत्य् एतद् आदर्शे तन् निराकृतम्

“If consciousness were transferred to the intellect like fire to an iron ball, that was refuted by the example of the face’s reflection in a mirror.”

Verse I.18.86

I.18.86

कृष्णायो रोहिताभासम् इत्य् एतद् दृष्टम् उच्यते । दृष्टदार्ष्टान्ततुल्यत्वं न तु सर्वात्मना क्वचित्

“Black iron appears red by reflecting fire; the example and what it illustrates are alike in that respect, not in every respect.”

Verse I.18.87

I.18.87

तथैव चेतनाभासं चित्तं चैतन्यवद् भवेत् । मुखाभासो यथादर्श आभासश् चोदितो मृषा

“In the same way the mind appears conscious through a reflection of consciousness; the image of a face in a mirror is also only an appearance.”

Verse I.18.88

I.18.88

चित्तं चेतनम् इत्य् एतच् छास्त्रयुक्तिविवर्जितम् । देहस्यापि प्रसङ्गः स्याच् चक्षुरादेस् तथैव च

“The claim ‘the mind is conscious’ lacks the support of scripture and reasoning; the same would then follow for the body, eye and the other senses.”

Verse I.18.89

I.18.89

तद् अप्य् अस्त्व् इति चेत् तन् न लोकायतिकसंगतेः । न च धीर् दृशिर् अस्मीति यद्य् आभासो न चेतसि

“If you accept that too, you arrive at materialism. But unless the intellect reflects awareness, how could it even have the thought ‘I am the Seer’?”

Verse I.18.90

I.18.90

सद् अस्मीति धियो ऽभावे व्यर्थं स्यात् तत् त्वम् अस्य् अपि । युष्मदस्मद्विवेकज्ञे स्याद् अर्थवद् इदं वचः

“If the cognition ‘I am Being’ were absent, the teaching ‘you are that’ would be pointless; these words are meaningful to one who knows the distinction between ‘you’ and ‘I.’”

Verse I.18.91

I.18.91

ममेदंप्रत्ययौ ज्ञेयौ युष्मद्य् एव न संशयः । अहम् इत्य् अस्मदीष्टः स्याद् अयम् अस्मीति चोभयोः

“Without doubt, the thoughts ‘mine’ and ‘this’ belong to ‘you’; ‘I’ belongs to ‘we’; and both are present in the thought ‘I am this.’”

Verse I.18.92

I.18.92

अन्योन्यापेक्षया तेषां प्रधानगुणतेष्यते । विशेषणविशेष्यत्वं तथा ग्राह्यं हि युक्तितः

“They are held to be principal and subordinate in relation to one another; the relation of qualifier and qualified is reasonable.”

Verse I.18.93

I.18.93

ममेदं द्वयम् अप्य् एतन् मध्यमस्य विशेषणम् । धनी गोमान् यथा तद्वद् देहो ऽहंकर्तुर् एव च

“‘Mine’ and ‘this’ together qualify the intermediate ego, as ‘wealthy’ and ‘owner of cattle’ qualify a person; so do the body and the ego.”

Verse I.18.94

I.18.94

बुद्ध्यारूढं सदा सर्वं साहंकर्त्रा च साक्षिणः । तस्मात् सर्वावभासो ज्ञः किंचिद् अप्य् अस्पृशन् सदा

“Everything always appears in the intellect and with the ego; therefore the knower, witness of all, reveals everything while never touching anything.”

Verse I.18.95

I.18.95

प्रतिलोमम् इदं सर्वं यथोक्तं लोकबुद्धितः । अविवेकधियाम् अस्ति नास्ति सर्वं विवेकिनाम्

“All this, explained in reverse of ordinary belief, exists only for minds that fail to discriminate; to the discerning, everything of this kind is absent.”

Verse I.18.96

I.18.96

अन्वयव्यतिरेकौ हि पदार्थस्य पदस्य च । स्याद् एतद् अहम् इत्य् अत्र युक्तिर् एवावधारणे

“Agreement and difference are the reasoning for the meaning of a thing and of a word; here they establish what is meant by ‘I.’”

Verse I.18.97

I.18.97

नाद्राक्षम् अहम् इति अस्मिन् सुषुप्ते ऽन्यन् मनाग् अपि । न वारयति दृष्टिं स्वां प्रत्ययं तु निषेधति

“In deep sleep, ‘I did not see anything else’ does not deny my own seeing; it denies the cognition.”

Verse I.18.98

I.18.98

स्वयंज्योतिर् न हि द्रष्टुर् इत्य् एवं संविदो ऽस्तिताम् । कौटस्थ्यं च तथा तस्याः प्रत्ययस्य च लुप्तताम् । स्वयम् एवाब्रवीच् छास्त्रं प्रत्ययावगती पृथक्

“Scripture itself declares that awareness is self-luminous and the Seer changeless, that the cognition disappears, and that the cognition and awareness are distinct.”

Verse I.18.99

I.18.99

एवं विज्ञातवाक्यार्थे श्रुतिलोकप्रसिद्धितः । श्रुतिस् तत् त्वम् असीत्य् आह श्रोतुर् मोहापनुत्तये

“When the ordinary meaning of the words has been understood from scripture and common usage, scripture says ‘you are that’ to remove the listener’s delusion.”

Verse I.18.100

I.18.100

ब्रह्मा दाशरथेर् यद्वद् उक्त्यैवापानुदत् तमः । तस्य विष्णुत्वसंबोधे न यत्नान्तरम् ऊचिवान्

“Just as Brahmā removed Daśaratha’s son’s darkness by words alone, without requiring further effort to make him recognize his nature as Viṣṇu,”

Verse I.18.101

I.18.101

अहंशब्दस्य या निष्ठा ज्योतिषि प्रत्यगात्मनि । सैवोक्ता सद् असीत्य् एवं फलं तत्र विमुक्तता

“so the essential meaning of the word ‘I,’ the inward light, is expressed as ‘you are Being’; its fruit is liberation.”

Verse I.18.102

I.18.102

श्रुतमात्रे न चेत् तत् स्यात् कार्यं तत्र भवेद् ध्रुवम् । व्यवहारात् पुरापीष्टः सद्भावः स्वयम् आत्मनः

“If that does not arise from merely hearing, some further task would surely be required; yet even before this, ordinary usage accepts the Self’s existence as one’s own.”

Verse I.18.103

I.18.103

अशनायादिनिर्मुक्त्यै तत्काला जायते प्रमा । तत्त्वमस्यादिवाक्यार्थे त्रिषु कालेष्व् असंशयः

“For one free from hunger and the rest, valid knowledge of the meaning of ‘you are that’ arises at that very moment, without doubt, in all three times.”

Verse I.18.104

I.18.104

प्रतिबन्धविहीनत्वात् स्वयं चानुभवात्मनः । जायेतैव प्रमा तत्र स्वात्मन्य् एव न संशयः

“Since there is no obstacle and awareness is itself one’s experience, valid knowledge surely arises there in one’s own Self.”

Verse I.18.105

I.18.105

किं सद् एवाहम् अस्मीति किं वान्यत् प्रतिपद्यते । सद् एव चेद् अहंशब्दः सता मुख्यार्थ इष्यताम्

“Does one then understand ‘I alone am Being,’ or something else? If the word ‘I’ means Being, let that be accepted as its primary meaning.”

Verse I.18.106

I.18.106

अन्यच् चेत् सदहंग्राहप्रतिपत्तिर् मृषैव सा । तस्मान् मुख्यग्रहे नास्ति वारणावगतेर् इह

“If it meant something else, the cognition ‘Being is what I grasp as myself’ would be false; therefore no barrier to the primary meaning remains here.”

Verse I.18.107

I.18.107

प्रत्ययी प्रत्ययश् चैव यदाभासौ तदर्थता । तयोर् अचितिमत्त्वाच् च चैतन्ये कल्प्यते फलम्

“Both the thinker and the thought are reflections; because neither is conscious, their result is imagined in consciousness.”

Verse I.18.108

I.18.108

कूटस्थे ऽपि फलं योग्यं राजनीव जयादिकम् । तदनात्मत्वहेतुभ्यां क्रियायाः प्रत्ययस्य च

“Even the changeless witness may be credited with a result, as a king is credited with victory, because action and cognition belong to what is not-Self.”

Verse I.18.109

I.18.109

आदर्शस् तु यदाभासो मुखाकारः स एव सः । यथैवं प्रत्ययादर्शो यदाभासस् तदा ह्य् अहम्

“The image in a mirror has the face’s form; in the same way, when a cognition appears in the mirror of the intellect, one thinks ‘I am.’”

Verse I.18.110

I.18.110

इत्य् एवं प्रतिपत्तिः स्यात् सद् अस्मीति च नान्यथा । तत् त्वम् इत्य् उपदेशो ऽपि द्वाराभावाद् अनर्थकः

“Thus the understanding ‘I am Being’ arises; otherwise, without this means of access, the instruction ‘you are that’ would be pointless.”

Verse I.18.111

I.18.111

श्रोतुः स्याद् उपदेशश् चेद् अर्थवत्त्वं तथा भवेत् । अध्यक्षस्य न चेद् इष्टं श्रोतृत्वं कस्य तद् भवेत्

“If the instruction is addressed to a hearer, it has meaning. If the witness is not a hearer, who would the instruction concern?”

Verse I.18.112

I.18.112

अध्यक्षस्य समीपे बुद्धेर् एवेति चेन् मतम् । न तत्कृतोपकारो ऽस्ति काष्ठाद् यद्वन् न कल्प्यते

“If you say it concerns the intellect because it is near the witness, the witness does not thereby help it, just as wood is not helped by mere proximity.”

Verse I.18.113

I.18.113

बुद्धौ चेत् तत्कृतः कश्चिन् नन्व् एवं परिणामिता । आभासे ऽपि च को दोषः सति श्रुत्याद्यनुग्रहे

“If the witness does something to the intellect, then the witness would change. What fault is there in saying it helps the reflection, with the support of scripture and the rest?”

Verse I.18.114

I.18.114

आभासे परिणामश् चेन् न रज्ज्वादिनिभत्ववत् । सर्पादेश् च तथावोचम् आदर्शे च मुखत्ववत्

“If the reflection itself changes, there is no problem: it is like the snake imagined in a rope. I have already explained the example of the face appearing in a mirror.”

Verse I.18.115

I.18.115

नात्माभासत्वसिद्धिश् चेद् आत्मनो ग्रहणात् पृथक् । मुखादेस् तु पृथक्सिद्धिर् इह त्व् अन्योन्यसंश्रयः

“The Self’s reflection is not established, because the Self is grasped separately; the face and its reflection are separately established, while here each depends on the other.”

Verse I.18.116

I.18.116

अध्यक्षस्य पृथक्सिद्धाव् आभासस्य तदीयता । आभासस्य तदीयत्वे ह्य् अध्यक्षव्यतिरिक्तता

“If the witness were established separately, the reflection could be said to belong to it; if the reflection belonged to it, the witness would have to be distinct from that reflection.”

Verse I.18.117

I.18.117

नैवं स्वप्ने पृथक्सिद्धेः प्रत्ययस्य दृशेस् तथा । रथादेस् तत्र शून्यत्वात् प्रत्ययस्यात्मना ग्रहः

“In a dream, however, neither the cognition nor the Seer is established separately: chariots and the rest are empty there, and cognition itself is grasped as the Self.”

Verse I.18.118

I.18.118

अवगत्या हि संव्याप्तः प्रत्ययो विषयाकृतिः । जायते स यदाकारः स बाह्यो विषयो मतः

“Pervaded by awareness, a cognition arises with the form of its object; whatever form it takes is considered an external object.”

Verse I.18.119

I.18.119

कर्मेप्सिततमत्वात् स तद्वान् कार्ये नियुज्यते । आकारो यत्र चार्प्येत करणं तद् इहोच्यते

“That object is what action most desires to obtain; an agent is directed toward its result. Where a form is placed [in the intellect], that is called the instrument.”

Verse I.18.120

I.18.120

यदाभासेन संव्याप्तः स ज्ञातेति निगद्यते । त्रयम् एतद् विविच्यात्र यो जानाति स आत्मवित्

“One pervaded by this reflection is called the knower. Distinguishing this triad, one understands the Self.”

Verse I.18.121

I.18.121

सम्यक्संशयमिथ्योक्ताः प्रत्यया व्यभिचारिणः । एकैवावगतिस् तेषु भेदस् तु प्रत्ययार्पितः

“Correct, doubtful and false cognitions vary; awareness within them is one, and the distinctions are attributed to the cognitions.”

Verse I.18.122

I.18.122

आधिभेदाद् यथा भेदो मणेर् अवगतेस् तथा । अशुद्धिः परिणामश् च सर्वं प्रत्ययसंश्रयात्

“As a jewel appears different because of the adjuncts around it, so impurity and change alike depend on cognition.”

Verse I.18.123

I.18.123

प्रथनं ग्रहणं सिद्धिः प्रत्ययानाम् इहान्यतः । आपरोक्ष्यात् तद् एवोक्तम् अनुमानं प्रदीपवत्

“The manifestation, grasping and establishment of cognitions arise from something other than the cognition; because it is immediate, this is described as inference, like a lamp.”

Verse I.18.124

I.18.124

किम् अज्ञं ग्राहयेत् कश्चित् प्रमाणेन तु केनचित् । विनैव तु प्रमाणेन निवृत्त्यान्यस्य शेषतः

“By what means of knowledge could someone make the ignorant one know? Without any means, removing the other [superimposition] alone leaves what remains.”

Verse I.18.125

I.18.125

शब्देनैव प्रमाणेन निवृत्तिश् चेद् इहोच्यते । अध्यक्षस्याप्रसिद्धत्वाच् छून्यतैव प्रसज्यते

“If one says scripture is the means by which this is removed, but the witness is not established, then emptiness alone follows.”

Verse I.18.126

I.18.126

चेतनस् त्वं कथं देह इति चेन् नाप्रसिद्धितः । चेतनस्यान्यतासिद्धाव् एवं स्याद् अन्यहानतः

Objection: “How can you be conscious and the body not?” The reply: “That is not established; if consciousness were established as other than the body, then only the not-Self would be removed.”

Verse I.18.127

I.18.127

अध्यक्षः स्वयम् अस्त्य् एव चेतनस्यापरोक्षतः । तुल्य एवं प्रबोधः स्याद् अज्ञस्यासत्त्ववादिना

“The witness exists by itself and consciousness is immediate; the ignorant person and the one who denies reality would be awakened in the same way.”

Verse I.18.128

I.18.128

अहम् अज्ञासिषं चेदम् इति लोकस्मृतेर् इह । करणं कर्म कर्ता च सिद्धास् त्व् एकक्षणे किल

“From the ordinary memory ‘I knew this,’ the instrument, action and agent appear to be established all at once.”

Verse I.18.129

I.18.129

प्रामाण्ये ऽपि स्मृतेः शैघ्र्याद् यौगपद्यं विभाव्यते । क्रमेण ग्रहणं पूर्वं स्मृतेः पश्चात् तथैव च

“Even if memory is valid, its speed makes them seem simultaneous; first there is the apprehension, and then the memory follows.”

Verse I.18.130

I.18.130

अज्ञासिषम् इदं मां चेत्य् अपेक्षा जायते ध्रुवम् । विशेषो ऽपेक्ष्यते यत्र तत्र नैवैककालता

“The thought ‘I knew this’ requires a relation of comparison; wherever a distinction must be considered, it is not all at once.”

Verse I.18.131

I.18.131

आत्मनो ग्रहणे चापि त्रयाणाम् इह संभवात् । आत्मन्य् आसक्तकर्तृत्वं न स्यात् करणकर्मणोः

“Even if all three are possible in the apprehension of the Self, agency cannot attach to the Self, instrument or object.”

Verse I.18.132

I.18.132

व्याप्तुम् इष्टं च यत् कर्तुः क्रियया कर्म तत् स्मृतम् । अतो हि कर्तृतन्त्रत्वं तस्येष्टं नान्यतन्त्रता

“The object is that which the agent wishes to reach through action; therefore it depends on the agent, not on itself.”

Verse I.18.133

I.18.133

शब्दाद् वानुमितेर् वापि प्रमाणाद् वा ततो ऽन्यतः । सिद्धिः सर्वपदार्थानां स्याद् अज्ञं प्रति नान्यथा

“For an ignorant person, all things are established by words, inference or some other means of knowledge; there is no other way.”

Verse I.18.134

I.18.134

अध्यक्षस्यापि सिद्धिः स्यात् प्रमाणेन विनैव वा । विना स्वस्य प्रसिद्धिस् तु नाज्ञं प्रत्य् उपयुज्यते

“The witness may be established with or without a means of knowledge, but its self-establishment does not help the ignorant person.”

Verse I.18.135

I.18.135

तस्यैवाज्ञत्वम् इष्टं चेज् ज्ञातत्वे ऽन्या मितिर् भवेत् । अन्यस्यैवाज्ञतायां च तद्विज्ञाने ध्रुवा भवेत्

“If the witness itself is said to be ignorant, then when it is known there must be another cognition; if something else is ignorant, then knowledge of that other is certain.”

Verse I.18.136

I.18.136

ज्ञातता स्वात्मलाभो वा सिद्धिः स्याद् अन्यद् एव वा । ज्ञातत्वे ऽनन्तरोक्तौ त्वं पक्षौ संस्मर्तुम् अर्हसि

“Is establishment knowledge, attainment of oneself, or something else? In speaking of knowledge, remember the two possibilities just mentioned.”

Verse I.18.137

I.18.137

सिद्धिः स्यात् स्वात्मलाभश् चेद् यत्नस् तत्र निरर्थकः । सर्वलोकप्रसिद्धत्वात् स्वहेतुभ्यस् तु वस्तुनः

“If establishment is attainment of oneself, effort is pointless: everyone already knows reality through its own causes.”

Verse I.18.138

I.18.138

ज्ञानज्ञेयादिवादे ऽतः सिद्धिर् ज्ञातत्वम् उच्यते । अध्यक्षाध्यक्ष्ययोः सिद्धिर् ज्ञेयत्वं नात्मलाभता

“In the view of knower and knowable, establishment is said to mean being known; the witness and its object are knowable, not attainment of oneself.”

Verse I.18.139

I.18.139

स्पष्टत्वं कर्मकर्त्रादेः सिद्धता यदि कल्प्यते । स्पष्टतास्पष्टते स्याताम् अन्यस्यैव न चात्मनः

“If clarity of the agent and the rest is imagined as establishment, that clarity and its absence belong to something else, never to the Self.”

Verse I.18.140

I.18.140

अद्रष्टुर् नैव चान्धस्य स्पष्टीभावो घटस्य तु । कर्त्रादेः स्पष्टतेष्टा चेद् द्रष्टृताध्यक्षकर्तृका

“A pot cannot become clear to one who does not see, who is blind. If the agent and the rest are made clear, they must be clear to a seer, the witness.”

Verse I.18.141

I.18.141

अनुभूतेः किम् अन्तस्मिन् स्यात् तवापेक्षया वद । अनुभवितरीष्टा स्यात् सो ऽप्य् अनुभूतिर् एव नः

“Tell me what further experience you expect. If an experiencer is required, for us that experiencer is itself experience.”

Verse I.18.142

I.18.142

"अभिन्नो ऽपि हि बुद्ध्यात्मा विपर्यासितदर्शनैः । ग्राह्यग्राहकसंवित्तिभेदवान् इव लक्ष्यते"

“Though intellect and Self are not distinct, those whose vision is reversed perceive them as though divided into grasped object and grasper.”

Verse I.18.143

I.18.143

[भूतिर् येषां क्रिया सैव कारकं सैव चोच्यते । ] सत्त्वं नाशित्वम् अस्याश् चेत् सकर्तृत्वं तथेष्यताम् । न कश्चिच् चेष्यते धर्म इति चेत् पक्षहानता

“[For those who hold that existence itself is an action and an instrument,] if existence is momentary, agency too must be admitted. If no property is accepted, that position loses its basis.”

Verse I.18.144

I.18.144

नन्व् अस्तित्वादयो धर्मा नास्तित्वादिनिवृत्तयः । न भूतेस् तर्हि नाशित्वं स्वालक्षण्यं हि ते

“Existence and the rest are properties, and nonexistence and the rest are their absences. If so, destruction cannot be a property of existence; those are its own characteristics.”

Verse I.18.145

I.18.145

स्वलक्षणावधिर् नाशो नाशो ऽनाशनिवृत्तिता । अगोर् असत्त्वं गोत्वं ते न तु तद् गोः स्वलक्षणम्

“Destruction is the limit of a thing’s own character; it is the ending of what does not last. A cow’s nonexistence is not its cowhood, which is its own characteristic.”

Verse I.18.146

I.18.146

क्षणवाच्यो ऽपि यो ऽर्थः स्यात् सो ऽप्य् अन्याभाव एव ते । भेदाभावे ऽप्य् अभावस्य भेदो नामभिर् इष्यते

“Even the thing called a moment is only the absence of something else; though absence is not different in itself, names make it seem divided.”

Verse I.18.147

I.18.147

नामभेदैर् अनेकत्वम् एकस्य स्यात् कथं तव । अपोहो यदि भिन्नानां वृत्तिस् तस्य कथं गवि

“How can differences of names make one thing many for you? If exclusion belongs to distinct things, how could that same process apply to the cow?”

Verse I.18.148

I.18.148

नाभावा भेदकाः सर्वे विशेषा वा कदाचन । नामजात्यादयो यद्वत् संविदस् ते ऽविशेषतः

“Differences never arise from absence or from qualities; names, classes and the rest are no different from awareness itself.”

Verse I.18.149

I.18.149

प्रत्यक्षम् अनुमानं वा व्यवहारे यदीच्छसि । क्रियाकारकभेदैस् तद् अभ्युपेयं ध्रुवं भवेत्

“If you want perception or inference to operate in ordinary dealings, then agency and its factors must certainly be accepted.”

Verse I.18.150

I.18.150

तस्मान् नीलं तथा पीतं घटादिर् वा विशेषणम् । संविदस् तद् उपेत्यं स्याद् येन चाप्य् अनुभूयते

“Blue, yellow and a pot are attributes of awareness, which receives them and by which they are experienced.”

Verse I.18.151

I.18.151

रूपादीनां यथान्यः स्याद् ग्राह्यत्वाद् ग्राहकस् तथा । प्रत्ययस्य तथान्यं स्याद् व्यञ्जकत्वाच् च दीपवत्

“Just as forms and the rest are distinct from the receiver because they are objects, thoughts too are distinct from their revealer, like things lit by a lamp.”

Verse I.18.152

I.18.152

अध्यक्षस्य दृशेः कीदृक् संबन्धः संभविष्यति । अध्यक्ष्येण तु दृश्येन मुक्त्वान्यो द्रष्टृदृश्यताम्

“What kind of relation can there be for the witness, awareness itself, other than being the Seer of what is seen?”

Verse I.18.153

I.18.153

अध्यक्षेण कृता दृष्टिर् दृश्यं व्याप्नोत्य् अथापि वा । नित्याध्यक्षकृतः कश्चिद् उपकारो भवेद् धियाम्

“Does the witness produce seeing, which then pervades what is seen? What benefit could the intellect receive from the eternally present witness?”

Verse I.18.154

I.18.154

स चोक्तस् तन्निभत्वं प्राक् संव्याप्तिश् च घटादिषु । यथालोकादिसंव्याप्तिर् व्यञ्जकत्वाद् धियस् तथा

“That benefit was described earlier as taking its likeness and pervading objects such as pots, just as light pervades what it reveals.”

Verse I.18.155

I.18.155

आलोकस्थो घटो यद्वद् बुद्ध्यारूढो भवेत् तथा । धीव्याप्तिः स्याद् घटारोहो धियो व्याप्तौ क्रमो भवेत्

“A pot in the light becomes present to the intellect; when the intellect pervades the pot, the pot is known. Thus the intellect’s pervasion takes place in sequence.”

Verse I.18.156

I.18.156
